Nine slicing splits the source image into a 3x3 grid — four corners, four edges, one center — and scales each region differently when the panel resizes: corners are repositioned but never scaled (so border art stays crisp), each edge stretches along only its own axis to connect the corners, and the center stretches on both axes to fill the middle. Requires only that the edge/center art be drawn so it tiles or stretches cleanly; built into most major engines as a first-class primitive (Godot's NinePatchRect/StyleBoxTexture, Unity's Sliced Sprite image type, and CSS's border-image-slice), so adopting it needs no custom tooling.
- [Nine-Step Juicy Damage Number Technique (Pop, Float, Fade, Arc, Scale, Color, Crit FX, Stack, Randomize)](entries/juicy-damage-numbers.md) — Game-dev technique for upgrading a default floating-damage-number label into "juicy" feedback through a stack of independent, optional tweaks rather than one fix — pick a legible display font (the reel uses Bungee from Google Fonts), pop the number in on spawn instead of just appearing, float it upward as it arrives, fade it out at the end of its lifetime, give each digit an arc-like falling offset instead of a straight rise, scale its size by the damage value so bigger hits read as bigger, shift its color over time based on which action produced it, layer particles and screen shake onto critical hits using the same logic, experimentally stack numbers on very fast multi-hit attacks, and randomize each number's spawn offset so a burst of hits doesn't render as a single overlapping stack. Framed as a menu of optional, independently-applicable steps rather than a required pipeline, and as engine-agnostic (the creator states it applies to any game engine).
- [Noise-Panning Foliage Animation (Perlin Noise Vertex Displacement)](entries/noise-panning-foliage-animation.md) — Game-dev technique for animating large numbers of static foliage/vegetation meshes cheaply by driving per-object or per-vertex movement from a Perlin noise texture instead of hand-animating each piece — pixel brightness sets how much an object moves, continuously panning the noise across the environment avoids visible looping, and layering several noise patterns at different speeds adds variation; turns one static art asset into apparently-living foliage across hundreds of instances without per-object animation clips.
- [Obsidian + Claude Code Second Brain Workflow](entries/obsidian-claude-second-brain-workflow.md) — Practitioner workflow for building an AI-native personal knowledge base using Obsidian as the storage layer and Claude Code as the structuring agent — vault organized as books/, articles/, posts/, wiki/ with frontmatter tags and wikilinks; CLAUDE.md defines the schema and operating manual; Claude reads raw text and structures it into tagged pages, wiki entries, comparison tables, and cross-referenced summaries without plugins or API wrappers
- [One-Person AI Company Framework](entries/one-person-ai-company-framework.md) — A practitioner's framework distilled from operating a business almost entirely on AI agents for six months from a single git repo, covering context-as-code, a routing brain, human approval queues, reversibility discipline, and a taste loop for improving agent output over time
- [Outcomes / Outcome Grader (Managed Agents)](entries/outcomes-managed-agents.md) — Demonstrates the Outcomes feature in Claude Managed Agents, which adds a rubric-driven verification loop where an independent grader — with no visibility into the writer's reasoning — scores an artifact and returns gaps for the writer to revise, up to a configurable iteration cap
- [Parallel Local Inference Pattern (vLLM)](entries/parallel-local-inference.md) — Strategy for maximizing local GPU throughput by running multiple concurrent LLM agents via vLLM — exploits idle compute from memory-bandwidth bottleneck; 8-9x aggregate throughput over single-stream; per-agent throughput drops slightly but total throughput scales near-linearly with concurrency
- [Persistence Keys for Entity State Across Area Unload/Reload (Seamless Streaming Worlds)](entries/persistence-key-entity-state.md) — Game-dev technique for keeping entity state (e.g. a defeated enemy) consistent when a seamless streaming world unloads and reloads the area containing it, without a full save/load pass. Problem: in games with invisible/seamless loading zones (as opposed to distinct rooms, where re-spawning enemies on re-entry reads as normal), an unloaded enemy's defeat is lost — walking back into a previously-cleared area respawns the enemy, breaking immersion. Fix: a small piece of state (a 'persistence key' / flag), identified by the entity's own name/ID, is written to an object that never unloads (e.g. the player character) at the moment the entity is defeated; when the area streams back in and the entity's scene re-instantiates, it checks the persistent object for its own key before running its normal spawn logic, loading in a defeated state if the key is present. Demonstrated in an indie 2D pixel-art game (enemy 'Runk Tonk', persistent holder 'Isadora') without naming a specific engine's API, though captioned with Godot tags. The same pattern generalizes to any stable-ID-keyed state delta kept outside the unloadable scene graph — analogous to dedicated streaming-persistence systems in other engines (e.g. Unreal's SPUD plugin and native LevelStreamingPersistenceManager) and to the broader save-system architecture principle of writing deltas keyed by stable IDs to a store decoupled from runtime objects, rather than serializing live scene state.
- [Production RAG as a System (8-Part Taxonomy)](entries/production-rag-system-taxonomy.md) — Practitioner taxonomy framing production RAG as an 8-part system (not a vector-database feature): 01 offline build time (parse/chunk/embed/index), 02 online query time (rewrite/retrieve/rerank/assemble/generate), 03 knowledge store (vectors+metadata+raw docs+freshness), 04 retrieval quality (hybrid search/filters/top-k/reranking), 05 answer quality (grounding/citations/structured output/fallback), 06 state/data layer (Postgres+Redis+object storage+vector index), 07 observability/evals (latency/recall/answer quality/hallucinations/cost), 08 safety operations (ACLs/PII protection/prompt-injection defense/caching/feedback); presented as a directory-tree mental model contrasting with the four-step chunk-embed-retrieve-prompt demo pipeline.
- [Prompt Assembly and Cache Discipline](entries/prompt-assembly-cache-discipline.md) — Rules for LLM prompt assembly that preserve KV-cache efficiency — never rewrite the front of a prompt (invalidates cached tokens), avoid sliding windows that evict oldest turns (same cost), cap fat messages in-place to stay cacheable, put retrieval/per-query content last as a suffix so changes do not poison the cached prefix; includes five-layer assembler concept and ablation methodology
- [Prompt Versioning and Rollback (Managed Agents)](entries/prompt-versioning-managed-agents.md) — Demonstrates prompt lifecycle management using Claude Managed Agents' versioning system — immutable version creation on every update, version pinning for controlled evaluation, labeled-test-set comparison between versions, and rollback by switching the pinned version number
- [Pseudo-3D via Sprite Billboarding Over 2D Gameplay Logic, with Fog-Based Distance Culling](entries/pseudo-3d-sprite-billboarding.md) — Game-dev rendering technique for making a fully 2D top-down game look 2.5D/3D without changing its gameplay logic. The camera is tilted down from a pure overhead view, and top-down sprites are 'stood up' and rotated to face the camera via a single matrix transform in code (classic sprite billboarding, as in Pokemon Black & White-style pseudo-3D). Hit detection stays simple circle hitboxes and elevation/jump checks stay a plain numeric comparison between grid cells, both unaware of the new camera angle. Tilting the camera low exposed a far draw distance that tanked frame rate; hiding distant geometry in fog and simplifying far props (trees) recovered performance, reported as a 7x speedup. Demonstrated in a devlog for the in-development game 'Soul Stream.'
- [Qwen 3.8 27B at 200K Context on 16GB VRAM (tutorial)](entries/qwen38-27b-200k-on-16gb.md) — Practitioner recipe for running Qwen 3.8 27B UD-IQ4_XS at ~196K context and 50 t/s on a single RTX 5070 Ti (16GB) — prune non-ASCII rows from the embedding table and LM head (~700MB, ASCII-condensed variant published), offload the embedding table to CPU, disable MTP, use adaptive-KV streaming (llama.cpp fork) to page KV cache from host RAM, and quantize cache Q8 K / Q4 V
- [RAG Architecture Decision Tree](entries/rag-architecture-decision-tree.md) — Diagnostic framework for choosing among 8 RAG architectures (Naive, Hybrid, Reranked, Multi-Query, Hierarchical, Corrective, Graph, Agentic) based on observed failure modes — treat RAG like debugging: let Naive fail first, watch how it fails, then pick the architecture that fixes that specific symptom; measure retrieval separately from generation
- [RAG Retrieval Recipe](entries/rag-retrieval-recipe.md) — Practitioner's RAG recipe — semantic chunking (500-1000 tokens, 100 overlap), embed once with nomic/mxbai, store in ChromaDB/Qdrant/LanceDB, retrieve top-20 → BGE rerank → top-5, cite source chunks in LLM output
- [Reusable Node Components for Shared Enemy/Entity Behavior (Godot Composition Over Inheritance)](entries/godot-node-composition-components.md) — Game-dev architecture technique for sharing behavior across many entity types without duplicating code or using deep inheritance chains: break each behavior into a small, self-contained node (e.g. a health component that stores health, subtracts damage, and fires a signal when it reaches zero; a hit-detection component; a damage-on-touch component; a death-drop component), each of which knows nothing about the specific enemy it's attached to. A new enemy is assembled by dropping in the needed components, wiring their signals, and tuning numbers — only animation work is enemy-specific. Improving a shared component improves every entity using it at once. This is Godot's established community pattern of composition over inheritance via nodes and signals, not a technique unique to one project; demonstrated in a dev-log reel where 76 enemies in the roguelike Capy of Gods (built in Godot) run on the same shared health component.
- [Rogue's 3x3 Grid ("Tic-Tac-Toe") Dungeon Generation Algorithm](entries/rogue-1980-tic-tac-toe-dungeon-generation.md) — Procedural dungeon-layout algorithm from Rogue (1980, by Michael Toy and Glenn Wichman, later joined by Ken Arnold), the game that gave the roguelike genre its name. Rather than placing rooms at arbitrary positions — which collide and can leave rooms unreachable — Rogue's source (version 5.4, files rooms.c and passages.c) divides its 80x24 terminal screen into a fixed 3x3 grid of nine cells. Each cell gets at most one room, randomly sized and positioned but confined to its cell so rooms can never overlap or share a wall; up to three cells (equally likely to be 0-3) are left empty, acting only as a point corridors pass through. Cells are then linked one at a time, each new link joining an already-connected cell to an unconnected neighbor, until all nine are reachable from the starting room, plus up to four extra random links so the layout isn't a single unique path. The result is a layout that is random but always fully connected and always resolvable within a single draw call's worth of simple grid math.
- [Scheduled Multi-Agent Coordinator Pattern](entries/scheduled-multi-agent-coordinator.md) — Useful architecture pattern for persistent scheduled agents with coordinator + messaging; NanoClaw and Hermes are mature implementations
- [SCRUB: A Framework for Context Engineering in Claude Code](entries/scrub-context-engineering.md) — Five-action framework (Subagents, Cut/rewind, Reduce/compact, Upload/handoff, Burn/clear) for proactively managing an agent's context window, from a practitioner report of ~3000 hours in Claude Code
- [Shadow-Mode Progressive Trust for Agent Approval Gates](entries/shadow-mode-progressive-trust-approval.md) — Review-load design pattern for human-in-the-loop agent approval: tier actions by risk/reversibility rather than by tool, run gated actions in shadow mode (agent logs what it would decide without executing, compared against the human's actual call) to measure per-action-type agreement rather than one global rate, write explicit promotion/demotion rules (agreement above threshold for N decisions with no high-severity miss loosens the gate a notch; a drop or miss tightens it) so oversight shrinks as the agent earns it instead of staying flat, keep approval UI to a few lines of what/why/evidence, and convert any judgment call the human keeps approving the same way into a deterministic code rule instead of re-reviewing it
- [Single-Agent Synchronous Workflow](entries/single-agent-synchronous-workflow.md) — Argument and configuration for denying the Agent tool in Claude Code — single-agent synchronous workflows preserve context evidence (subagent summaries lose the backing data), enable mid-run interruption, and improve cache efficiency; adversarial review via in-repo documentation compliance agents is the endorsed exception
- [SIRA — Superintelligent Retrieval Agent](entries/sira-superintelligent-retrieval-agent.md) — Meta research framework that compresses multi-round agentic retrieval into a single corpus-discriminative BM25 action — LLM enriches documents offline with missing vocabulary, predicts query-side evidence terms, validates via IDF tool calls, then issues one weighted sparse retrieval; outperforms dense retrievers and multi-round agents on BEIR/NQ/HotpotQA with no fine-tuning
- [Skill Orchestrator Shell (Instruction-Shell Pattern)](entries/skill-orchestrator-shell.md) — Composition pattern: group thematically related agent skills and add a thin orchestrator skill that only routes and sequences between them and enforces explicit sign-off checkpoints, keeping the actual task instructions inside the individual skills rather than the orchestrator.
- [Software Engineering Fundamentals for AI Agents (schema-first memory, deterministic guardrails)](entries/agent-software-engineering-fundamentals.md) — Practitioner discussion arguing most agent memory, prompt-injection, and goal-hijacking problems are solved by pre-LLM software-engineering basics — design the memory data model first (what is stored, how it changes behavior, TTL and scope) instead of free-text dumps; treat LLM output as untrusted input validated against schemas; model the agent as a workflow (defined state + allowed transitions, model proposes moves that code checks); least-privilege scoped credentials, tool allowlists, process/data isolation, audit logs; reserve human approval for irreversible or boundary-crossing actions and show the exact payload to avoid approval fatigue
- [Software Factory Orchestration (DAG-Based Dev Lifecycle Automation)](entries/software-factory-orchestration.md) — Pattern for automating the full software development lifecycle with a deterministic workflow DAG — spec writing → ticket generation → per-ticket plan/execute/review → final code review — using coding agents as nodes and a workflow engine (n8n, Airflow, or custom bash/TS script) as the orchestrator; addresses opacity and steering problems of pure multi-agent approaches
- [Start Cheap, Escalate on Fail: Measured Model/Effort Routing](entries/start-cheap-escalate-on-fail-routing.md) — A measured cost-routing strategy for coding agents: define a task as work with a pass/fail test (the SWE-bench model — FAIL_TO_PASS plus PASS_TO_PASS), always attempt it first on the cheapest model/effort tier, run the test, and only escalate model/effort on failure. Demonstrated on 6 SWE-bench tasks (2 quick/medium/hard) across Sonnet, Opus, and Fable at varying effort: on one quick task, Fable at max effort cost $2.10/~5min while Sonnet at low effort cost $0.07/14s and both passed the identical test. Even counting the cost of failed escalation attempts, starting cheap came out cheaper across all six tasks tested. Suggests logging (task features, tier tried, pass/fail, cost) and fitting a classifier (e.g. scikit-learn) on that log to build a task router that predicts where to start for a given task shape.
- [Storing Per-Pixel Time and Location in a Texture to Drive Ground Effects (Instead of Spawned Decals/Actors)](entries/texture-time-ground-effects.md) — Game-dev technique for rendering ground effects (e.g. area-of-effect visuals) without spawning a decal or actor object per effect instance. Time-of-occurrence and location data is written into a texture; a material reads that texture to control how the effect visually appears and disappears over time. A separate data-driven grid system tracks which grid cells a character currently occupies, used to decide when to trigger or clear an effect, rather than each effect instance independently tracking its own lifetime and overlap with other effects. Reported benefit is avoiding the lifetime/overlap bookkeeping cost that scales with the number of independently-spawned decal/actor effects. Demonstrated in Unreal Engine by the two-person dev team of the upcoming roguelike 'Barrow and Blade' as one of three non-standard optimization techniques.
- [Tailscale Mobile Approval Workflow](entries/tailscale-mobile-approval.md) — Pattern for approving Claude Code permission prompts from a mobile device via Tailscale SSH + tmux — attach to a persistent session from any device on the tailnet, review diffs and approve remotely
- [The 7 Levels of Observability](entries/seven-levels-of-observability.md) — A content creator's seven-stage observability maturity ladder — logs, metrics, traces, correlation (shared request ID across logs/traces), alerts, SLOs (SLI/SLO/SLA), and cardinality management — presented as a progression where each stage answers a question the previous one couldn't (what happened, is it trending, where is the time going, why, before users notice, is the target at risk, can we afford to measure it). Not a single named industry-standard framework: published observability maturity models elsewhere range from 3 pillars (logs/metrics/traces) to 4-5 level maturity scales (e.g. Last9, codefarm.in) covering the same underlying concepts — structured logs with trace IDs, OpenTelemetry-style distributed tracing, SLO/error-budget-driven alerting, and cardinality limits on metric labels — under different level counts and names.
- [Tileless Terrain Rendering via Per-Pixel Four-Corner Blending Shader](entries/tileless-terrain-corner-blend-shader.md) — Game-dev terrain-rendering technique that replaces hand-drawn tileset art entirely with a single shader. Each map cell stores one terrain-type number instead of a chosen tile sprite; a shader reads the four cell values surrounding each screen pixel and renders a terrain layer at that pixel only when it covers more than half of those four corners, producing automatically-rounded coastlines, grass/dirt edges, and other transitions with no pre-drawn edge, corner, or inner-corner art. The same four-corner read drives layered procedural detail (grass blades, foam, roads, footpaths, plowed fields, snow) and the entire ground renders in one draw call; demonstrated in the dev log for Valdrin, a 2D medieval kingdom-sim on Steam.
- [Token-Budget Workflow for Small-Context Local Coding Agents](entries/token-budget-local-agent-workflow.md) — Practitioner workflow for making a heavily quantized local LLM (32K-65K context) usable as a coding agent despite its narrow context window and higher error rate: (1) pick a harness with a small startup token footprint (author reports Pi needs ~1.5K tokens vs. an estimated 10-14K for Codex/Claude Code, leaving more of a 64K window for actual work) instead of a heavier, feature-rich harness; (2) split every task into a planning turn that writes plan.md, then clear the session (Pi's /new) and run a separate implementation turn that reads plan.md and records results in it, so neither turn needs the whole task history in context; (3) for plans too large for one implementation turn, delegate via a subtask-runner skill (subtask4llm) that executes up to 10 subtasks sequentially in fresh, isolated agent contexts, feeding results back to the coordinating instance; (4) compensate for the model's lower quality at low-bit quantization by enforcing hard constraints in AGENTS.md — a 300-500 line file-size ceiling, one responsibility per module, mandatory unit tests for every function/change, tests-and-build must pass before done, no commits without explicit user request — which the author attributes to a remark by Robert Martin (Uncle Bob, author of Clean Code) that he has stopped reading AI-generated code, substituting enforced constraints for manual review and spending free local-GPU iterations letting the model self-correct against those constraints.
- [Tracer Bullets / Vertical Slices for Agent Plans](entries/tracer-bullets-vertical-slices.md) — Matt Pocock's argument (via thedailyai.news clip) that AI coding agents fail on horizontal layer-by-layer plans (database → API → frontend) for lack of an integrated feedback loop; the fix is the Pragmatic Programmer 'tracer bullet' idea — thin vertical tasks that cross database, service, and frontend to prove the whole flow, reframing work from 'complete phase 1' to 'ship one visible, testable slice'
- [Trinity: Evolved LLM Coordinator](entries/trinity-coordinator.md) — Sakana AI paper (ICLR 2026) on a 0.6B coordinator trained via evolutionary strategy (sep-CMA-ES) to orchestrate pools of LLMs through multi-turn role assignment (Thinker/Worker/Verifier); 86.2% on LiveCodeBench, zero-shot transfer to unseen tasks. Companion to Conductor (RL approach).
- [Uber Agentic Pods](entries/uber-agentic-pods.md) — An organizational pattern for deploying AI agents at enterprise scale via a 10-day sprint pipeline — shadowing employees, selecting opportunities, building the agent alongside the worker, validating with the team, then shipping — reporting reductions like 2 days to 10 minutes for financial pacing reports
- [VS Code Port Forwarding via Dev Tunnels (Public Localhost Sharing)](entries/vscode-port-forwarding-devtunnels.md) — Technique for sharing a locally-running dev server with anyone over the internet using VS Code's built-in Ports panel — run the project, note the port, open the Ports view, use Forward a Port, then open the generated URL's context menu and set Port Visibility to Public so viewers reach it without GitHub sign-in. The underlying mechanism is Microsoft Dev Tunnels (URLs resolve to *.devtunnels.ms); forwarded ports default to Private (requires sign-in with the same GitHub account used to start forwarding) and must be explicitly switched to Public to work as a no-login share link. Works for any local server (the reel demonstrates a full-stack Vite/React PDF-tools clone) as long as the process keeps running on the local machine — the tunnel relays traffic, it does not host the app independently.
- [Webcam Feedback Loop for Agent Hardware/Firmware Debugging](entries/webcam-feedback-loop-hardware-debug.md) — Anecdote of a coding agent (Opus 5.5 via Claude Code) suggesting its own instrumentation for a hardware task it couldn't otherwise observe: pointing a webcam at a physical ESP32-driven display so the agent could see the live rendered output while tuning Waveshare display-driver settings for portrait-mode frame rate, going from 4fps to 60fps in a few minutes once it had that visual feedback loop.
- [You cannot automate a habit (discovery before automation)](entries/you-cannot-automate-habit.md) — Practitioner reflection on building business automations/agents: the hard part is rarely the model or framework — it's that the target process only exists as an undocumented habit. Ask three people how it works and you get three answers (owner's original design, manager's exception-laden version, the operator's notebook-based reality); even 'the menu' has no single answer (per-outlet prices, time-of-day availability, duplicate names). The reusable practice: before automating, sit with people, force the business to write its own rules down for the first time, and get someone with authority to sign off on the version that will be encoded.

### cli-tool

- [Adeptly](entries/adeptly.md) — Plan-first companion GUI for Claude Code — drafts structured plans with inline Claude Code feature recommendations (subagents, skills, hooks, MCP, plan mode), scores feature coverage and token hygiene, then optionally executes plans as a role pipeline (Architect→Builder→Medic→Reviewer→Security→Pilot) via headless claude CLI; local-first Next.js app, MIT
- [Agent Browser (Vercel Labs)](entries/agent-browser.md) — Rust-native browser automation CLI for AI agents — snapshot-based accessibility tree with ref selectors, MCP server mode, annotated screenshots, multi-session isolation, plugin system, cloud provider integrations (Browserbase/Browserless/AWS AgentCore); Apache-2.0, Vercel-backed
- [Agent Reach](entries/agent-reach.md) — Capability-layer CLI that gives AI agents internet access — routes to best backend per platform (Twitter/Reddit/YouTube/Bilibili/Xiaohongshu/GitHub/LinkedIn/RSS/web/search), auto-installs tools, health-checks with doctor command; cookie-based auth, self-described vibe-coded, agent-self-install pattern carries supply-chain risk; MIT
- [AgentTrail — local observability dashboard for AI coding agents](entries/agenttrail.md) — Local, open-source observability layer for coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and any tool that edits files), shipped as two independent npm-installed views in one repo. "Map" (`npx agenttrail`) combines a PLAN.md convention with observed file changes into a live component map with dependency links and session trails; a completed component re-lights when its files change again, and `npx agenttrail init` can scaffold the plan and wire optional Claude Code hooks. "Kitchen" (`npx agenttrail-kitchen .`, public experimental preview) is a separate Three.js 3D view that turns native agent todos and local Codex/Claude session logs — plus optional Cursor hooks — into a cooking-themed board (chefs, tickets, delivery). Both services bind to 127.0.0.1 only, require no account or cloud calls, and do not launch or control agents, only observe local files and logs. Map does not yet parse Claude Code's newer TaskCreate/TaskUpdate todo format (only legacy TodoWrite); native Cursor live behavior and Windows are unverified per the project's own FAQ. JavaScript, MIT license, single contributor (114 commits), 968 GitHub stars and 70 forks as of this review; created 2026-08-21, last pushed 2026-09-09 (no commits in the ~7 weeks before this review).
- [Aider](entries/aider.md) — Terminal AI pair-programming tool (Python) — edits code in a local git repo via chat with cloud or local LLMs, builds a repo map for large codebases, auto-commits each change with sensible messages, runs linters/tests after edits; 100+ languages, voice-to-code, image/web-page context; Apache-2.0, ~49k stars, last pushed May 2026
- [AiZynthFinder](entries/aizynthfinder.md) — AiZynthFinder performs retrosynthetic planning — given a target molecule, it finds multi-step synthetic routes back to commercially available starting materials using Monte Carlo tree search with reaction templates suggested by a trained neural network policy
- [Aleph](entries/aleph-geo.md) — Python CLI/dashboard (alephgeo) that downloads satellite imagery, terrain elevation, OSM buildings, Street View photos, and textured 3D meshes for any drawn area or named place, with no API key; built to give LLMs a local, structured snapshot of real-world geography; MIT license; pulls undocumented Google APIs so may break or rate-limit.
- [Amazon Textract Textractor](entries/amazon-textract-textractor.md) — Textractor is a Python wrapper around Amazon Textract for document intelligence — handles API calls, response parsing, and output formatting for text recognition, table extraction, form extraction, ID analysis, and expense processing
- [Aperture CLI](entries/aperture-cli.md) — Tailscale's CLI launcher for coding agents (Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Codex, Copilot CLI, OpenCode) preconfigured for Aperture — manages installation, config, env vars, and model/provider routing; Go, alpha
- [Atuin](entries/atuin.md) — Shell history replacement backed by SQLite — records exit code, duration, cwd, hostname, session per command; full-screen search UI; encrypted sync across machines via Atuin server (self-hosted or cloud); supports bash/zsh/fish/nushell/xonsh/PowerShell; Rust; MIT
- [AutoDock Vina](entries/autodock-vina.md) — AutoDock Vina predicts how small molecules bind to protein targets by searching for the lowest-energy binding pose, supporting virtual screening, multi-ligand docking, macrocycle and hydrated-docking protocols, and both AutoDock4.2 and Vina scoring functions
- [BreachDirectory](entries/breachdirectory.md) — Free breach lookup engine — searches billions of breached credentials and personal records by email, username, or domain; shows plaintext and hashed passwords from real data dumps; API available via RapidAPI (free basic tier, paid pro); dual-use OSINT tool for breach monitoring and credential exposure checking; proprietary, web-based
- [Brogue: Community Edition](entries/brogue-ce.md) — Community-maintained fork of Brian Walker's Brogue roguelike — seeded procedural dungeon generator that places rooms by sliding candidates until a door meets an existing wall without overlap, cuts extra doors between far-apart floor areas to add loops, and shapes lakes with a 5-round cellular-automaton smoothing pass gated by a connectivity check; C, SDL2, 26 playable levels, 1.5k stars/197 forks; AGPL-3.0.
- [Bubblewrap (containers)](entries/bubblewrap-containers.md) — Low-level unprivileged Linux sandboxing tool using user namespaces — used by Flatpak, creates empty mount namespaces with selective filesystem binding; C, LGPL-2.0+, mature and auditable
- [Bubblewrap (PWA)](entries/bubblewrap-pwa.md) — Google Chrome Labs CLI for wrapping Progressive Web Apps as Android Trusted Web Activity (TWA) APKs — Node.js toolchain with core library, CLI, and validator; Apache-2.0
- [caire](entries/caire.md) — Go content-aware image resizing library and CLI implementing seam carving — computes an edge-energy map, repeatedly finds and removes (or duplicates) the lowest-cumulative-energy one-pixel-per-row seam to shrink or grow an image without uniform squeezing; optional face-detection and protective/removal masks to guide which regions survive; ~11k GitHub stars, MIT
- [Career-Ops](entries/career-ops.md) — Career-Ops turns any AI coding CLI into a job search command center that scores job offers against a user profile, generates ATS-optimized CVs and cover letters as PDFs, scans career portals automatically, and tracks everything in a markdown-based pipeline
- [Chandra OCR](entries/chandra-ocr.md) — Chandra OCR 2 is a vision-language model for document parsing that converts images and PDFs into structured Markdown/HTML/JSON while preserving layout, with strength on math, tables, forms, handwriting, and 90+ languages; code is Apache-2.0, model weights use a modified OpenRAIL-M license
- [claude-pee](entries/claude-pee.md) — PTY wrapper driving interactive CLI; inherently brittle, breaks on CLI version changes
- [CleanYourTV](entries/cleanyourtv.md) — Debloats Android TV / Google TV over network ADB without root — backs up package state, disables manufacturer telemetry, ad rows and preloads with pm disable-user (nothing uninstalled, one-line restore), and builds/installs a ~532 KB plain-Java launcher sized for 1 GB TVs; driven by a coding agent (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI) via PROMPT.md/AGENTS.md or by hand with bash/PowerShell scripts and a pure-Python ADB client; per-model debloat lists for 170+ TV models from 200+ contributors; Python/Java, MIT (launcher font OFL)
- [Cobalt](entries/cobalt.md) — General-purpose media downloader with a self-hosted API; no agent, skill, or bioinformatics relevance.
- [CodeBurn](entries/codeburn.md) — Cross-tool AI spend observability dashboard — reads local session files from 31+ tools (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, etc.), breaks down tokens and cost by task/model/project, waste detection with copy-paste fixes, model comparison, git-correlated yield tracking; MIT, fully local
- [CodeQL](entries/codeql.md) — GitHub's semantic SAST engine — compiles source into a queryable relational database and runs CodeQL-language queries (SQL injection, XSS, etc.) against it without executing the code; CLI integrates into GitHub Actions/Advanced Security code scanning and can fail CI on a match; 12 languages; CLI under GitHub's own Terms (free for OSI-licensed public repos/academic use, GHAS license required for private repos), query packs MIT
- [cpubrrr](entries/cpubrrr.md) — From-scratch CPU-only LLM inference engine in Rust that beats llama.cpp's CPU path on MoE models. Hand-written NEON/SME kernels, quad-interleaved weight layout, integer-accumulation Q8_K, worker-driven execution. Runs gpt-oss:20b at ~77 tok/s and Qwen3-Coder-30B at ~92 tok/s on Apple M4 Max (CPU only). OpenAI-compatible API server included. Dual MIT/Apache-2.0.
- [Demucs](entries/demucs.md) — Hybrid Transformer music source separation model from Alexandre Défossez — splits a song into drums, bass, vocals, and other stems via dual waveform/spectrogram branches joined by a cross-domain transformer; MIT licensed, pip/uvx installable, 9.0 dB SDR on MUSDB HQ
- [Distill](entries/distill.md) — Local 1.7B model pipe-filter for CLI output compression is a real problem but requires 8–16 GB RAM and relies on an unaudited HuggingFace model; overlaps with rtk.
- [dlaf](entries/dlaf.md) — Michael Fogleman's C++ command-line tool implementing diffusion-limited aggregation (DLA) — random walkers stick together on contact with a growing cluster, producing coral- or lichen-like branching structures in 2D or 3D; computes roughly one million points per core in ~35 seconds (per its own README) using Boost for spatial indexing, and writes particle ID/parent/coordinate data to CSV, leaving rendering to the caller; last pushed 2020-01-09; MIT.
- [doom-fire-algorithm](entries/doom-fire-algorithm.md) — Filipe Deschamps's playground of ~70 community reimplementations of the DOOM fire effect across JS/Rust/C++/Python/Go/Unity/Godot/etc. — a grid of 0-36 values mapped to a 37-color palette, bottom row pinned to 36, each cell takes the value below minus a random 0-2 (shifted up to two cells left) so the fire cools and flickers as it rises; 1.5k stars, 304 forks, actively maintained (pushed 2026-10-17); MIT.
- [dumbtv](entries/dumbtv.md) — Removes ads, tracking and clutter from Amazon Fire TV sticks, Cubes and Fire TV Edition televisions over ADB without root — disables ~50 Amazon packages (sponsored rows, ad services, ACR screen fingerprinting, telemetry, experiments) with pm disable-user, turns off ad tracking and usage reporting, optionally runs the open-source personalDNSfilter on the TV to block ~400 Amazon telemetry/ad/update hosts, and replaces Amazon's home screen with a 54 KB permissionless Java launcher; agent-driven via AGENTS.md (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Gemini) or by hand via preflight/configure/all/verify/restore bash scripts; nothing written to /system, one-command undo; MIT scripts and launcher, CC BY-SA 4.0 device catalog
- [ergon-wrong-turn-logger](entries/ergon-wrong-turn-logger.md) — CLI that logs development 'wrong turns' (bad assumptions, missed edge cases, broken designs) to a searchable local SQLite database, with list/stats/search commands; framed for solo, team, and AI-assisted workflows.
- [Firejail](entries/firejail.md) — SUID Linux sandbox in C — restricts untrusted applications via namespaces, seccomp-bpf, capabilities, and optional Landlock/AppArmor/SELinux; 1,300+ bundled per-application profiles, desktop integration via firecfg, no daemon; GPL-2.0, ~7.6k stars, actively maintained since 2015
- [Free My TV](entries/free-my-tv.md) — Open-source, ad-free home-screen replacement for Google TV/Android TV, LG webOS and Samsung Tizen TVs — a glass-shelf launcher showing only the owner's own apps, hourly TMDB trending picks limited to installed apps, Continue Watching from the TV's own Watch Next list, weather/crypto cards, a matching screensaver, and a sunset-driven day/night theme; runs through the TV's own ADB (pm disable-user/pm grant/pm revoke, cmd package set-home-activity) with nothing rooted or uninstalled and a one-button "Original" revert; agent-driven install (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity given the TV's IP and city) builds and adb-installs it, with a prebuilt, signature/SHA-256-verified auto-updating APK also offered for Google TV; Java/Python/plain JS, MIT.
- [fzf](entries/fzf.md) — General-purpose command-line fuzzy finder and interactive terminal toolkit — processes millions of items in milliseconds; event-driven architecture for custom workflows; shell integration (CTRL-R history, CTRL-T files, ALT-C directories) for bash/zsh/fish/nushell; preview window with image support; popup mode (tmux 3.3+/Zellij); single Go binary; MIT
- [Glass Home for Samsung Tizen TV](entries/glass-home-samsung-tv.md) — Apple TV-style home screen for Samsung Tizen TVs (2018-2025, Tizen 4-10) — a frosted-glass app dock, crossfading nature photos, clock/weather (Open-Meteo), and a screensaver, paired with a small password-protected website the TV itself serves on the home LAN for uploading photos and adding website/YouTube shortcuts from a phone; sideloaded via Tizen Developer Mode with a free per-TV Samsung certificate (no universal download, no root); one Tizen package bundles the UI and a Node.js service on 127.0.0.1:8090; ships tvremote.py (stdlib client for the TV's local control API), phonecam.py (turns a phone into a camera pointed at the TV, since "Samsung TVs can't take screenshots"), and wake.py; plain HTML/CSS/JS, MIT.
- [godogen](entries/godogen.md) — Generator + agent-driven workflow for autonomous game development targeting Godot 4, Bevy, or Babylon.js — a publish.sh script renders a thin runtime layout (manifest, engine guide, asset-generation skill) into a fresh target repo for either Claude Code (CLAUDE.md + .claude/skills/) or Codex (AGENTS.md + .agents/skills/), then the chosen host agent builds the actual game by reading the engine guide and calling external asset-generation APIs (Gemini, xAI Grok, Tripo3D)
- [Google Workspace CLI (gws)](entries/googleworkspace-cli.md) — Rust CLI with agent-first JSON output and 100+ bundled SKILL.md files covering all Workspace APIs; pre-v1.0 with breaking changes expected
- [Granola CLI](entries/granola-cli.md) — A Go CLI that exports data from the Granola meeting note-taking app to local files for backup, migration, or offline access, covering both API-based notes export (ProseMirror JSON to Markdown) and cache-based transcript export with speaker labels
- [Helm (gamepad-driven multi-agent session manager)](entries/helm-session-manager.md) — Electron desktop app (Windows .exe, macOS universal .dmg) for running and switching between multiple AI coding CLI sessions (Claude Code, Copilot CLI, Codex CLI, etc.) as embedded terminals (node-pty + xterm.js), controlled with a game controller or keyboard; includes an opt-in per-session prompt-cache keep-warm heartbeat (auto-off after 8 hours), a Helm MCP server so agents can control sessions programmatically, cross-machine "Fleet" pairing with deny-by-default per-peer allow-lists, a Telegram bot for remote control, a per-folder plan graph, pattern-matched auto-fire on terminal output, scheduled prompts, a project memory knowledge graph with scheduled "dreaming" maintenance, and session mission/todo/ETA tracking with compaction handover notes; an Android companion app is sideloaded via APK. No LICENSE file in the repository. Single-repo project.
- [Herdr](entries/herdr.md) — Terminal-based agent multiplexer (single Rust binary) — real terminal panes for many coding agents at a glance, detach/reattach across terminals or SSH with sessions surviving restarts, tmux-style keyboard + mouse, a plugin marketplace, and a pure socket API that lets agents themselves spawn panes and wait on each other; dual-licensed AGPL-3.0 / commercial
- [hotdog](entries/hotdog.md) — Zero-dependency TypeScript/Bun AI agent harness with tool calling — connects to any OpenAI-compatible chat-completions endpoint (local llama-swap/llama.cpp/vllm or cloud), with hook-driven extensions, profiles, skills, MCP client, subagent tasks, and an optional --sandbox mode restricting tools to non-destructive ones; MIT, single-maintainer hobby project
- [Instaloader](entries/instaloader.md) — Python CLI and library that downloads public and private Instagram profiles, hashtags, stories, feeds, and saved media along with captions, comments, and geotags — supports incremental re-sync (`--fast-update`, `--latest-stamps`) and session-cookie login for private content; MIT licensed, ~13.7k GitHub stars as of 2026
- [jq](entries/jq.md) — Lightweight command-line JSON processor — slice, filter, map, and transform structured data; portable C with zero runtime dependencies; sed/awk/grep for JSON; MIT
- [Kasetto](entries/kasetto.md) — Declarative environment manager for AI coding agents — one YAML config syncs skills, MCPs, commands, and instructions across 23 agents (Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, etc.) with lockfile pinning, secret injection, and per-agent format translation; Rust binary
- [KDE Connect](entries/kde-connect.md) — Free, open-source app (GPLv2/v3) that pairs a phone and computer over the same local Wi-Fi network for direct device-to-device sync — no account, no cloud relay, no vendor servers in the data path; shared clipboard, instant file/link/SMS transfer, phone-as-touchpad/presentation-remote, and browsing phone storage over SFTP; official Linux/Android support plus Windows (Microsoft Store or .exe) and macOS (Homebrew cask) builds, with a more limited native iOS app
- [lazydraw](entries/lazydraw.md) — Terminal ASCII diagram/sketch editor modeled on ASCIIFlow — mouse-driven box/arrow/line/text/eraser tools in a full-screen TUI (OpenTUI), multi-format export (basic/extended chars, 11 comment styles, Markdown fence), undo/redo, autosaving named drawings; Bun runtime, MIT, single-maintainer, npm-only (GitHub repo unavailable)
- [lazygit](entries/lazygit.md) — Terminal UI for git — interactive staging (hunks/lines), interactive rebase (squash/fixup/drop/edit/reorder), cherry-pick, bisect, worktrees, custom patches, undo/redo via reflog, commit graph, diff comparison, GitHub PR integration; Go; MIT
- [llmfit](entries/llmfit.md) — Terminal tool that detects local hardware (RAM/CPU/GPU/VRAM) and scores LLM models across fit, speed, quality, and context to report which will run well locally; ships an interactive TUI and a JSON-emitting CLI for agent/script use.
- [mapgen4](entries/mapgen4.md) — Procedural wilderness map generator — real-time terrain regeneration as you paint; Delaunay/Voronoi mesh with 25K+ cells; elevation, rivers, rainfall, biomes; oblique projection rendering; TypeScript/JavaScript; Apache 2.0
- [MarkItDown](entries/markitdown.md) — directly useful for converting PDFs, Excel, PPTX, and other docs to markdown for LLM pipelines; MIT, Microsoft-maintained
- [mergekit](entries/mergekit.md) — Toolkit for merging pre-trained LLMs — 16+ merge methods (SLERP, TIES, DARE, DELLA, task arithmetic, linear, Karcher mean, etc.); out-of-core merging on CPU or with 8GB VRAM; MoE construction from dense models; LoRA extraction; evolutionary merge methods; multi-stage merging; tokenizer transplantation; Frankenmerging (layer assembly); EMNLP 2024 paper; LGPL-3.0
- [mex](entries/mex.md) — Structured markdown scaffold + 11-checker drift-detection CLI for persistent AI agent memory; provider-neutral, ~60% token reduction claims, but single-contributor and overlaps with kb wiki + built-in memory
- [Microsoft Activation Scripts (MAS)](entries/microsoft-activation-scripts.md) — Open-source Windows and Office activator using HWID, Ohook, TSforge, and Online KMS methods — batch-script-based; HWID for permanent Windows 10/11 activation, Ohook for permanent Office activation, TSforge for permanent Windows/ESU/Office, Online KMS for 180-day renewable activation; includes edition changing and troubleshooting; v3.12 (July 2026)
- [motionbricks-blender](entries/motionbricks-blender.md) — Free, unofficial Blender add-on that imports motion generated by NVIDIA MotionBricks (.npz files) as an animated SOMA character, with optional greybox cubes, checker floor, sun, and follow camera; bundles a "walker" script that generates a walk along a JSON-defined waypoint course using no input motion clips at all, and a "recorder" script that captures MotionBricks' own Scene Preview demo to .npz; ships an AGENTS.md so a coding agent (Codex, Claude Code) can set up the full MotionBricks + add-on pipeline from one prompt; requires Blender 4.2+ and an NVIDIA RTX GPU (MotionBricks' TensorRT dependency); add-on GPL-3.0-or-later, walker/recorder scripts Apache-2.0; first release (v0.1.0) 2026-10-28.
- [netscanner](entries/netscanner.md) — Rust TUI network scanner and diagnostic tool — lists hardware interfaces, WiFi scanning with signal strength charts, IPv4 CIDR ping with hostname/OUI/MAC resolution, TCP/UDP/ICMP/ARP packet dump with pause/filter, open port scanning, traffic counting with DNS records, CSV export; requires root; built on Ratatui + libpnet; Linux/macOS/Windows (Npcap); MIT, ~1.8k stars
- [oauth-cli-coder](entries/oauth-cli-coder.md) — OAuth-based CLI automation; adds auth complexity without solving durability
- [olmOCR](entries/olmocr.md) — AI2's VLM-based PDF/image-to-Markdown toolkit — 7B parameter model converts PDFs with equations, tables, handwriting, multi-column layouts into clean Markdown; <$200/M pages; multi-node S3-coordinated batch pipeline; ships comprehensive benchmark (7K+ test cases); Apache 2.0
- [OpenDataLoader PDF](entries/opendataloader-pdf.md) — Top-benchmarked open-source PDF parser with bounding boxes, formula extraction, and RAG-ready output — worth piloting for bioinformatics literature ingestion before committing.
- [OpenWiki](entries/openwiki.md) — LangChain CLI that generates and maintains agent-oriented codebase documentation in an openwiki/ directory; auto-updates via CI (GitHub Actions / GitLab CI) on each push; appends references to AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md so coding agents discover the wiki; supports OpenRouter, Fireworks, Baseten, OpenAI, Anthropic, and OpenAI-compatible endpoints
- [Orca (ADE)](entries/orca-ade.md) — Agent Development Environment for running parallel coding agents (Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Pi, 30+ others) side-by-side — each in its own git worktree, tracked in one place; Ghostty-class terminal splits, Design Mode (Chromium click-to-prompt), annotate AI diffs, GitHub/Linear integration, SSH worktrees, mobile companion (iOS/Android), CLI for scripting; Electron, MIT
- [OWASP ZAP](entries/owasp-zap.md) — Dynamic application security testing (DAST) tool that attacks a running web app from outside — intercepting proxy plus automated/passive scanners for SQL injection, XSS, CSRF, etc.; GUI or headless daemon mode with a REST API for CI/CD automation; Apache-2.0, no paid tier; forked from Paros, became an OWASP project in 2010, Checkmarx-sponsored since Sep 2024 as 'ZAP by Checkmarx' while remaining independently governed; ~15k GitHub stars
- [Oxc (Oxidation Compiler)](entries/oxc.md) — Rust-based JavaScript/TypeScript toolchain from VoidZero (Evan You) providing a shared parser, linter (Oxlint), formatter (Oxfmt), transformer, minifier, and module resolver; Oxlint targets ESLint compatibility with 870+ built-in rules, claims 50-100x ESLint's speed, and offers type-aware linting via tsgo (the Go port of the TypeScript compiler). MIT.
- [PDF24 Creator](entries/pdf24-creator.md) — Free Windows desktop app and virtual printer from Geek Software GmbH (Berlin, developed since 2006) for merging, splitting, compressing, converting, OCR'ing (via Tesseract), signing, password-protecting, watermarking, and rearranging PDFs — offline, with no feature limits or paid tier; proprietary freeware funded by ads on PDF24's companion web tools; one patched local-privilege-escalation CVE in its MSI installer
- [PearWebOS](entries/pearwebos.md) — Python CLI and home-screen replacement for rooted LG webOS TVs, styled after Apple TV's Home — name/date/clock/weather over the owner's own photos, six apps in a dock with every other installed app one press down, Inputs/Settings top right; runs entirely through the TV's own rooted SSH access with a one-command revert to LG's stock Home; built for webOS 6 (2021 TVs), with newer webOS (incl. 10) supported as a separate app install rather than a full Home replacement.
- [Playwright CLI](entries/playwright-cli.md) — Microsoft-maintained CLI interface to Playwright browser automation (npm package @playwright/cli), pitched as more token-efficient than Playwright MCP for coding agents since it avoids loading MCP tool schemas and full accessibility trees into model context; installs as a Claude Code / GitHub Copilot skill
- [Pylint](entries/pylint.md) — Static code analyzer for Python that checks for errors, enforces coding standards, and flags code smells and refactoring opportunities without executing the code.
- [Qwen Code](entries/qwen-code.md) — Alibaba's open-source terminal coding-agent CLI, forked from Gemini CLI and adapted for the Qwen3-Coder model family on agentic coding tasks — asks permission before file edits by default, integrates Git conversationally, reads files as needed without manual context-adding, and auto-switches to vision-capable models when images appear in input; installable via npm (@qwen-code/qwen-code), Homebrew, or from source; Qwen OAuth free-tier auth; companion qwen-code-action GitHub Action and open-computer-use MCP service; TypeScript, Apache-2.0, ~29k GitHub stars
- [Revealer.US](entries/revealer-us.md) — OSINT platform for username, email, and phone lookup across 800+ live sources (social, gaming, forum, developer platforms) plus breach and stealer-log dataset search; results arrive as each source responds; AI Deep Search (paid) follows reused handles and writes cited reports with confidence scores; REST API for programmatic access; SaaS with free demo tier, paid from $12.99/mo; domain registered January 2026, low third-party trust score (27/100 Gridinsoft)
- [ripgrep](entries/ripgrep.md) — Line-oriented recursive regex search tool — respects .gitignore by default, skips hidden/binary files; SIMD-accelerated Rust regex engine; 3-30x faster than grep/ag/ack on benchmarks; PCRE2 opt-in for look-around; searches compressed files, multiple encodings; static binary; dual MIT/UNLICENSE
- [rtk — CLI Proxy for LLM Token Reduction](entries/rtk.md) — Directly reduces Claude Code token costs 60-90% via transparent Bash hook; Windows hook requires WSL but filters work natively
- [Ruff](entries/ruff.md) — Rust-written Python linter and formatter from Astral; re-implements 900+ rules (Flake8 + dozens of plugins, isort, pydocstyle, pyupgrade) and a Black-compatible formatter behind one interface, with built-in caching, autofix, pyproject.toml config, and editor/pre-commit/GitHub Action integrations. MIT.
- [seb-ocr](entries/seb-ocr.md) — Vision-language pipeline for turning scanned historical pages into structured datasets — Gemini-based OCR with adaptive rate limiting, sliding-window entity extraction via Pydantic-constrained JSON, incremental caching, and semantic deduplication via embedding cosine similarity; single-script Python, MIT
- [Semgrep](entries/semgrep.md) — Fast open-source static analysis tool using AST pattern matching to find bugs and security issues across 30+ languages; runs as a CLI, pre-commit hook, IDE integration, or CI step; LGPL-2.1 Community Edition engine with per-function-only taint tracking, commercial AppSec Platform adds cross-file/cross-function dataflow and 20,000+ proprietary rules; forked as Opengrep (also LGPL-2.1) after licensing concerns
- [shan-shui-inf](entries/shan-shui-inf.md) — Procedurally-generated infinitely-scrolling Chinese landscape paintings in SVG — mathematical models for mountains and trees using noise functions; runs in the browser; pure JavaScript; inspired by traditional Chinese landscape scrolls
- [Shodan](entries/shodan.md) — Search engine for internet-connected devices — crawls all public IPv4 addresses weekly, indexes service banners/vulnerabilities/exposed services; search filters for ports, protocols, products, OS, geo, vulns; CLI and REST API for programmatic access; Shodan Monitor for real-time network exposure alerts; data streaming firehose; 3M+ registered users including 89% of Fortune 100; free tier, one-time $49 membership, subscriptions from $69/mo; proprietary
- [Shrimply](entries/shrimply.md) — Cross-platform GPU-accelerated video editor in Rust — GTK 4 / libadwaita UI, Skia + wgpu + Slang + CUDA rendering, FFmpeg media, PipeWire audio, Python compute server; requires NVIDIA or Apple Silicon GPU; pre-alpha; GPL-3.0+
- [Skills CLI (skills.sh)](entries/skills-cli.md) — The Skills CLI (npx skills) is a package manager for agent skills — modular SKILL.md packages — providing discovery, installation, update checking, and scaffolding, backed by the skills.sh registry which ranks skills by total installs and verifies quality via install count, source reputation, and GitHub stars
- [SkillSpector (NVIDIA)](entries/skillspector.md) — NVIDIA open-source security scanner for AI agent skills — scans SKILL.md files, folders, repos, and libraries for prompt injection, credential theft, data exfiltration, remote scripts, hidden persistence, and MCP tool poisoning; returns 0–100 risk score; installable as MCP server
- [Skylos](entries/skylos.md) — Local-first static analysis CLI (Python, TS/JS, Java, Go, Kotlin, PHP, Rust, Dart, C#, Shell) that scans for dead code, security flaws, secrets, CI/CD misconfig, quality regressions, and AI-generated code defects; ships as CLI, GitHub Action, VS Code extension, and MCP server
- [splashsurf](entries/splashsurf.md) — Rust surface reconstruction library and CLI (Fabian Löschner, Interactive Computer Graphics) that converts SPH particle data (e.g. from [[splishsplash]]) into a smooth triangle mesh for rendering — loads formats including .bgeo and .vtk; 309 stars, 29 forks; MIT.
- [Stirling PDF](entries/stirling-pdf.md) — Self-hosted REST API for 50+ PDF operations enables privacy-preserving agentic PDF workflows without external services
- [SYBA](entries/syba.md) — SYBA classifies organic compounds as easy- or hard-to-synthesize using a Bayesian fragment scoring approach, decomposing each molecule into ECFP4-like fragments scored by their relative frequency in easy- versus hard-to-synthesize reference sets
- [Syncthing](entries/syncthing.md) — Mature, audited, decentralized file sync daemon — production-grade for agent artifact persistence and cross-machine data sharing.
- [SynFrag](entries/synfrag.md) — SynFrag predicts whether a molecule is easy or hard to synthesize using a two-stage AttentiveFP graph neural network — pretrained on 9.18M unlabeled molecules via fragment-assembly generation, then fine-tuned on 800K labeled molecules — with attention-heatmap interpretability
- [termDRAW](entries/termdraw.md) — Agent-friendly ASCII diagram editor for the terminal — retained vector-style objects (lines, boxes, text) with rearranging, fenced Markdown export, native .td.json documents for re-editing, clipboard via OSC 52, embeddable OpenTUI components; Bun 1.3+, MIT
- [terrain-erosion-3-ways](entries/terrain-erosion-3-ways.md) — Daniel Andrino's Python/NumPy demo comparing three procedural terrain-with-erosion techniques — iterative hydraulic-erosion simulation (rain gathers, flows downhill, carries sediment), a GAN trained on ~180k USGS elevation samples, and a river-network-first approach using Poisson disc sampling + Delaunay triangulation; last pushed 2022-11-22; MIT.
- [Tesseract OCR](entries/tesseract.md) — Open-source OCR engine (`libtesseract`) and CLI (`tesseract`), originally developed at HP and later Google, now maintained by Stefan Weil and Zdenko Podobny — LSTM-based neural line recognition in v4+ with the legacy v3 pattern-matching engine retained for compatibility, 100+ languages with UTF-8 output in plain text/hOCR/PDF/TSV/ALTO/PAGE formats; ~77.2k stars.
- [tfm — Terminal File Manager](entries/tfm-tui.md) — Mouse-first terminal file manager with places sidebar, grid view, drag-and-drop, image/video thumbnails (kitty graphics protocol), dual pane, embedded terminal, network locations (sftp/smb/WebDAV/FTP via gvfs), XDG trash, archive operations, tabs, 30+ themes, TypeScript plugin system; Linux only, built with Bun; MIT
- [Thunderjar](entries/thunderjar.md) — Comparative testing framework for coding agents — holds three of four variables (codebase state, prompt configuration, model, harness) constant and varies the fourth, then applies objective measurements (compilation, test pass, file assertions, token consumption, lint compliance, correctness). For evaluating whether prompt/model/harness changes actually improve agent output. Early-stage project by dwjohnston.
- [tiktoken](entries/tiktoken.md) — OpenAI's fast BPE tokenizer library — 3-6x faster than HuggingFace tokenizers; supports all OpenAI model encodings (o200k_base, cl100k_base, etc.); Python API for encode/decode, model-to-encoding lookup, custom encoding extension via plugin mechanism; educational submodule for visualizing BPE procedure; MIT
- [tldr-pages](entries/tldr-pages.md) — Community-maintained simplified man pages — practical examples for common CLI commands; covers UNIX/Linux/macOS/FreeBSD/Windows/Android/Cisco IOS; official clients in Python, Rust (tlrc), and Node.js; web client with offline PWA; 40+ language translations; CC BY 4.0
- [trigger-tree](entries/trigger-tree.md) — Documentation-discovery telemetry for Claude Code / Codex. Measures whether CLAUDE.md directives change agent behavior via deterministic local probes — per-directive adherence rates, file/folder heat maps, A-F docs-health grade, CI discoverability gate. All local, stdlib-only Python, zero tokens for measurement. Claude Code plugin (/tt) and standalone CLI (pipx install trigger-tree). MIT.
- [Trivy](entries/trivy.md) — Aqua Security's all-in-one security scanner (Go) — finds vulnerabilities (OS packages + language dependencies), IaC misconfigurations, secrets, and licenses across container images, filesystems, git repos, VM images, Kubernetes clusters, and AWS accounts; generates and scans SBOMs (SPDX/CycloneDX); single static binary, offline-capable DB, CI-native; Apache-2.0, ~38k stars
- [TUIOS — Terminal UI OS](entries/tuios.md) — Terminal multiplexer and window manager built in Go on Charm's Bubble Tea v2 — vim-like modal interface, BSP/scrolling/master-stack tiling, 9 workspaces, kitty graphics protocol passthrough (flicker-free video, sixel experimental), daemon mode with session resurrection, vim-style copy mode with 10K-line scrollback, tape scripting DSL for automation, command palette, app launcher, SSH server mode, web terminal mode; event-driven rendering (zero idle CPU); MIT
- [usbtree](entries/usbtree.md) — Cross-platform Rust TUI for live USB device tree inspection — enumerates hubs/devices/classes/speeds via nusb (no root, no libusb), hot-plug watch with timestamped event log, live per-device activity sparklines and bandwidth graphs (Linux), safe eject, PCI view, filter/yank; single static binary (~1.5 MB), zero runtime deps; Linux/macOS/Windows; MIT
- [Vidnote](entries/vidnote.md) — Self-hosted video-lecture-to-markdown converter with slide screenshots (Whisper + PySceneDetect); 'heavily vibe-coded' single-purpose tool — useful concept for certification-course capture but low polish and bus-factor 1
- [VoxelSpace](entries/voxelspace.md) — Sebastian Macke's JavaScript/Python reimplementation of the Comanche (1992) terrain-rendering trick — reads a height map and a pre-lit color map, projects one map point per screen column per scanline, and draws vertical lines front-to-back with distance-scaled sampling steps; no polygons; MIT (game-derived maps excluded from the license); 6.8k stars, 302 forks.
- [vphone-cli](entries/vphone-cli.md) — Virtual iPhone on Apple Silicon Mac — uses Apple's Virtualization.framework and PCC research VM infrastructure to boot full iOS (up to iOS 27) as a VM; firmware patching, restore, and VM lifecycle management; GUI window with app/file browsing, clipboard, screenshots, recording; local HTTP/WebSocket API for automation (screenshots, touch, swipes, hardware keys); guest control daemon with Irisin package manager; macOS 15+, Apple Silicon only; MIT, ~12k stars
- [webos-debloat (lucataco)](entries/lucataco-webos-debloat.md) — Single shell script for a rooted LG webOS TV that hides launcher bloat tiles and background apps, stops those apps from preloading into RAM, and disables home-screen promotions, sports alerts, recommendations and LG's Live Plus (ACR) tracking by editing LG's own app block list, preload-manager policy and settings keys (never the root filesystem), making every change reversible without blocking firmware updates; tested on one 2026 LG OLED (webOS 26, firmware 11.2).
- [yt-dlp](entries/yt-dlp.md) — Feature-rich command-line audio/video downloader supporting thousands of sites — a community-maintained fork of youtube-dl with faster multi-threaded downloads, advanced format selection, subtitle embedding, and a plugin system for custom extractors/postprocessors; ~200k GitHub stars, releases roughly every 2 weeks; Unlicense (public domain); flagged by the music-industry group IFPI for EU piracy watchlist consideration, though IFPI itself notes its open-source/public-domain nature makes it hard to remove.
- [Zoetrope](entries/zoetrope.md) — Rust TUI that visualizes Claude Code sessions as live flow graphs — reads JSONL transcripts from ~/.claude/projects/, shows agents/subagents/workflows as nodes with tool calls, status, and token counts; time-travel scrubbing, live following, gap compression, agent detail panels with prompts and tool timings; also runs in-browser via WASM; zero network, read-only; MIT
- [zoxide](entries/zoxide.md) — Smarter cd command — learns which directories you use most frequently for instant jumps; frecency-based ranking; interactive selection via fzf; supports bash/zsh/fish/nushell/PowerShell/elvish/xonsh/tcsh/POSIX; imports from autojump/z/fasd/atuin; Rust; MIT

### framework

- [ACE-Step 1.5](entries/ace-step.md) — Open-source local music-generation foundation model (successor to the original ACE-Step) — a hybrid architecture where a language model plans a song blueprint (metadata, lyrics, captions via chain-of-thought) that guides a Diffusion Transformer decoder; generates a full song in under 2 seconds on an A100 and under 10 seconds on an RTX 3090, runs locally on under 4GB VRAM (base models; the newer 4B-parameter XL variants need 12-20GB+), supports 10-second to 10-minute durations, batch generation of up to 8 songs, cover generation, repaint/edit, track separation, vocal-to-BGM, reference-audio-guided generation, LoRA personalization from a handful of songs, and 50+ languages; ships a Gradio UI, REST API, Python API, CLI, and a C++/GGML VST3 plugin; co-led by ACE Studio and StepFun; MIT, with a usage disclaimer about copyright/cultural-sensitivity risk in generated output.
- [Actual Budget](entries/actual-budget.md) — Local-first open-source personal finance app with envelope budgeting — NodeJS, sync across devices, self-hosted (Docker/Fly.io/PikaPods) or local desktop apps (Windows/Mac/Linux); community-driven with i18n via Weblate; MIT
- [AdGuard Home](entries/adguard-home.md) — Production-ready network-wide DNS ad/tracker blocker — infrastructure tool, not an agent/dev workflow component
- [Agent Dispatcher — repository retrieval and specialist-role routing for Claude Code and Codex](entries/agent-dispatcher.md) — Claude Code plugin and Codex skill pack that routes a task to one of 27 specialist roles (engineering, design, testing, research, writing, operations) and assembles a bounded context packet of source excerpts using symbols, source text, code relationships, Git history, and stack-trace frames — rather than a full-repo dump. Ships 79 bundled local skills, references 31 external skills, 8 workflow recipes, a registry of 19 MCP servers, and 50 detection signals for role/skill selection. Standard retrieval runs locally with no model or network calls; optional features (model-assisted reranking, an external 'Jev' decision-engine API, an Onboarding Explorer, procedural learning from recorded outcomes) are off by default and require the operator's own provider configuration. Installs via `claude plugin marketplace add` + `claude plugin install` for Claude Code, or `python3 install_codex.py` for Codex (installs one skill to `~/.agents/skills/agent-dispatcher/`). Invoked with `/agent-dispatcher:agent-dispatcher <request>` (Claude) or `$agent-dispatcher <request>` (Codex); roles can be forced directly (e.g. `$agent-dispatcher reviewer`). Verification is explicit: each role defines what evidence counts as done and reports which checks ran, passed, or were left unverified. Requires Python 3.10+, Git, and an authenticated Claude Code or Codex; native Windows is unsupported (WSL recommended). MIT license, single maintainer, repo created 2026-09-19 with ~50 GitHub stars and 4 forks as of this review.
- [AgentChassis — contract-first orchestration for long-running coding agents](entries/agentchassis.md) — Contract-first orchestration that splits agentic coding into non-overlapping orchestrator/worker/reviewer roles under one rule: the agent that defines the work cannot implement it. Every task is a durable work record stating scope, acceptance criteria, and validation before any code is written; a task missing those is rejected before any model runs (deterministic check, no model call). Workers are confined to their declared file scope (Linux bwrap sandbox where available); reviewers check each change against its contract; every run records honest enforcement provenance (enforced true/false plus isolation backend). Parallel agents run on non-overlapping scopes without collision, interrupted tasks resume from their record, and the repo accumulates an engineering record of what was planned, why, and whether it passed. Drives multiple agent CLIs (Codex, Claude, and more) through a typed MCP interface. Source-available under Elastic License 2.0 as public @agent-chassis/* npm packages; an optional hosted Chassis Control Engine (CCE, private beta) adds org-level admission and signed attestation.
- [AgentConnect](entries/agentconnect.md) — Open-source multi-agent platform where teams and AI agents collaborate across Slack, Telegram, Discord, Lark, GitHub, GitLab, Gitea, and Linear — agents can call one another, carry memory/skills, and work from messages, issues, PRs, webhooks, or schedules; supports Claude Code, Codex, Grok Build, DeepSeek, Pi, and any ACP-compatible runtime; self-hosted Docker/K8s; Apache-2.0
- [Agentic Applications for Unified Data Foundation Solution Accelerator](entries/microsoft-agentic-unified-data-foundation-fabric.md) — Microsoft solution accelerator that layers Microsoft Foundry agents + Agent Framework orchestration over a Microsoft Fabric unified data foundation (SQL Database in Fabric), enabling natural-language querying across governed enterprise datasets via a web front-end; ships configurable scenario packs (retail sales analysis, financial-services client-meeting prep) with synthetic sample data; MIT
- [AI Job Search](entries/ai-job-search.md) — AI-powered job application framework built on Claude Code. Fork-and-own workflow: /setup (self-profiling from documents or interview), /scrape (multi-portal search with fit scoring), /apply (drafter-reviewer pipeline producing tailored LaTeX CV + cover letter with PDF verification loop and ATS text-layer check). Also ships /interview prep, /rank batch scoring, /outcome tracking, /gmail-sync, /notion-sync, /html-report dashboard, /upskill gap analysis, /expand competency enrichment, /add-portal and /add-template generators. Danish job portals built-in; LinkedIn and freehire.me for other markets; /add-portal scaffolds new portals. MIT.
- [AirLLM](entries/airllm.md) — Clever layer-shard inference to run 70B models on 4GB VRAM, but maintenance has stalled since August 2024 while better-maintained alternatives (Ollama/llama.cpp) have matured.
- [Amazon Bedrock](entries/amazon-bedrock.md) — AWS managed platform for building generative AI applications — 100+ foundation models (Amazon Nova, Anthropic Claude, OpenAI GPT-5.x, Meta Llama, DeepSeek, Mistral, etc.) through one API; AgentCore for production agent deployment with any framework (LangChain, OpenAI Agents SDK, Claude Agent SDK, Strands); Knowledge Bases for RAG; Guardrails; model distillation, prompt caching, intelligent routing; enterprise security (SOC, ISO, FedRAMP High, HIPAA eligible)
- [Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Gateway](entries/bedrock-agentcore-gateway.md) — Fully managed AWS service that converts REST APIs and Lambda functions into MCP-compatible tool servers — zero-code MCP creation from OpenAPI/Smithy specs, semantic tool discovery, OAuth inbound + IAM/API-key/OAuth outbound auth, CloudWatch observability; solves M×N agent-to-tool integration at enterprise scale
- [Amazon S3 Vectors](entries/amazon-s3-vectors.md) — AWS cloud object storage with native vector support — 'vector buckets' hold vector indexes (up to 10,000 per bucket, up to 2 billion vectors per index at GA) queryable via dedicated APIs with no infrastructure to provision, up to 90% lower cost than purpose-built vector databases per AWS; metadata key-value filtering per vector; SSE-S3 or SSE-KMS encryption with per-index customer-managed keys; natively integrated with Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases and Amazon OpenSearch Service; announced in preview July 2025, reached general availability December 2, 2025 with a 40x per-index scale increase over preview.
- [ArmorOCR](entries/armorocr.md) — Two-stage adversarial OCR framework built on Qwen3-VL-8B — trains robust grounded text perception via on-policy self-distillation from transformed observations, then refines with GRPO and task-conditioned rewards for localization, recognition, spotting, and VQA; includes AdvSpot benchmark (390 images, 13 adversarial OCR types)
- [ArtCraft (storytold "Crafting Apps")](entries/artcraft.md) — Desktop IDE for interactive AI image/video creation (Windows/macOS releases, Linux build-from-source) integrating ~62 third-party generative models (Nano Banana, GPT Image, FLUX, Seedream, Kling, Veo, Sora, Suno, Hunyuan 3D, Marble, etc.) for 2D/3D compositing, character posing, kitbashing, and text/canvas-driven image and video editing; flagship of the storytold GitHub org's Crafting Apps line (70+ repos — PhotoCraft, LightCraft, FilmCraft, VectorCraft, PdfCraft, FigCraft, EffectCraft, SoundCraft, CADCraft, WordCraft, and reported Office clones), each billed as a clean-room from-scratch-in-pure-Rust reimplementation of professional creative/office software; reported as built largely by one developer (Brandon Thomas) using Claude Opus 5.5 over days in late Sept/Oct 2026; drew disputed/scrutinized viral GitHub-star growth and AI-slop criticism; ArtCraft itself dual-licensed MIT/Apache-2.0
- [ASI-Evolve](entries/asi-evolve.md) — Impressive autonomous research-loop results but arbitrary code execution per round, no stated license, and very new (2026 academic repo)
- [ASKCOS](entries/askcos.md) — ASKCOS is an integrated platform for computer-aided synthesis planning that combines ML models for retrosynthetic analysis, forward reaction prediction, reaction condition recommendation, synthetic complexity scoring, and fast reaction plausibility filtering; code is MPL-2.0, data/models are CC BY-NC-SA (noncommercial)
- [Autoharness](entries/autoharness.md) — needs eval benchmarks first; pilot when we have eval criteria for our skills/agents
- [Autoresearch](entries/autoresearch.md) — Highly influential autonomous experimentation loop pattern, but ML-training-specific — transferable idea, not transferable code
- [AX (Google)](entries/ax.md) — Google's open-source declarative orchestrator for AI agent workloads on Kubernetes — four Kubernetes-style primitives (Task, Workspace, Gateway, Model) run agents as sandboxed, suspendable actors on top of Agent Substrate; Go, Apache-2.0, gRPC + Redis control plane, kubectl-shaped CLI; early-stage/pre-stable
- [Axolotl](entries/axolotl.md) — Open-source LLM post-training framework — YAML-driven pipeline for SFT, LoRA/QLoRA, DPO/KTO/ORPO, GRPO, reward modeling, pretraining, multimodal (vision/audio); ND parallelism (CP+TP+FSDP), ScatterMoE fused LoRA, MoE expert quantization, QAT, sequence parallelism; built-in agent docs CLI; Apache 2.0
- [BDH (Dragon Hatchling)](entries/bdh.md) — Post-transformer neural architecture from Pathway — biologically inspired sparse graph of neuron-like nodes with Hebbian working memory, scale-free topology, and GPU-friendly state-space formulation; matches GPT-2-scale transformers at 10M-1B parameters with interpretable activations; 97.4% on Sudoku Extreme (internal implementation) without chain-of-thought; runs on NVIDIA AI infrastructure and AWS
- [Beam (Reflection AI)](entries/beam-reflection-ai.md) — 501B-total/23B-active sparse mixture-of-experts open-weight model from Reflection AI (a Brooklyn lab founded by two ex-Google-DeepMind researchers) for coding, reasoning, and agentic workloads — 1M-token effective context, pretrained on 23.8T tokens plus 100M+ RL rollouts; vendor-reported benchmarks include 81.6% SWE-bench Verified, 80.1% Terminal-Bench 2.1, 90.6% GPQA Diamond, 97.7% AIME 2026; weights (BF16/FP8/NVFP4) and a DFlash2 speculative-decoding drafter released under Apache-2.0 on 2026-10-16 with a 40+ partner hosting ecosystem
- [Benzi](entries/benzi.md) — Code-intelligence agent/tool that compiles a codebase into a queryable index (tree-sitter-based: symbols, call edges, references, class inheritance) before answering questions or editing, instead of grepping/globbing raw source into an LLM's context window. Ships as a VS Code extension, a headless CLI (benzi-headless), and an MCP server (benzi-mcp) for other agents, sharing one login. The VS Code/CLI agent additionally runs a runtime tracer, gates edits against a fresh parse, checks blast radius before/after writes, self-escalates to a larger model mid-task, and keeps persistent per-repo memory with one-step rollback. Covers 10 languages (Python deepest) plus an HTML/CSS/DOM-JS markup engine; 35+ query tools. Free during beta, BYOK against Anthropic/OpenAI/DeepSeek/Groq/Kimi/compatible APIs; self-published 82.4% SWE-bench Verified result. The GitHub repo is documentation only (no source), with GitHub reporting no recognized open-source license (license key: other/NOASSERTION).
- [BitNet (bitnet.cpp)](entries/bitnet-cpp.md) — Microsoft's official inference framework for 1-bit LLMs (BitNet b1.58) — runs 100B models on a single CPU at human reading speed (5-7 tok/s), 2-6x faster than FP16 with 55-82% energy reduction; optimized kernels for x86/ARM CPU and GPU
- [Blaxel](entries/blaxel.md) — Cloud infrastructure for autonomous agents — persistent microVM sandboxes with ~25ms resume, Agent Drive distributed filesystem, MCP server hosting, outbound firewalling, model gateway; SOC 2/HIPAA/ISO 27001, YC-backed, $7.3M seed from First Round
- [Blender](entries/blender.md) — Free, open-source 3D creation suite (modeling, sculpting, UV/texturing, rigging, animation, simulation, the Cycles and EEVEE render engines, compositing, video editing, and Grease Pencil 2D-in-3D drawing); scriptable via a Python API that drives the entire tool including a built-in Python console and add-on system; cross-platform (Linux, Windows, macOS); GPL-licensed (sources vary between citing GPL-2.0-or-later and GPL-3.0), free since 1.0 and open-source since 2002 after a community buyout from the original commercial owner (NaN); maintained by the Blender Foundation with an LTS release track (5.2 LTS released 2026-07-14); third-party MCP servers exist that let AI agents drive Blender's Python API over the Model Context Protocol, which is the specific integration referenced (and dismissed) in this entry's source item.
- [Boltz API (BoltzMol-1 / BoltzProt-1)](entries/boltz-api.md) — Boltz's biomolecular design API — BoltzMol-1 (small-molecule hit discovery, 6/10 targets with <51 compounds each) and BoltzProt-1 (de novo nanobody design, 3x hit rate over BoltzGen); $0.025/prediction, Python/JS SDKs, first-party Claude Code integration
- [bonsai-ada-surgery (Ternary Bonsai 2 27B on 8-12GB + agent layer)](entries/bonsai-ada-surgery.md) — Serving recipe and launcher for PrismML's Ternary Bonsai 2 27B (1.58-bit ternary GGUF, PTQ1_0 quant, 5.9GB) that runs the full 262,144-token trained context window on 8GB and 12GB NVIDIA cards — patched PrismML llama.cpp fork, a launcher that sizes the K/V cache per card (q4_0 on 12GB, q3_0 on 8GB), MTP speculative drafting, weights unchanged; fixes reported agent/coding regressions at the runtime layer (template-rejected effort words, output caps that cut off reasoning, a runaway "xhigh" reasoning default, tool calls returned as JSON) rather than the compression; optional agent "layer" (API cards, sandboxed Python) ported from the same author's mirai-s-ada; repo's own title claims 262k context on 12GB at 54-97 tok/s decode, 2x prefill, q8_0 KV recipe, grammar tool calls 9/9 (self-reported); OpenAI-compatible API; MIT for repo code, PrismML weights Apache-2.0.
- [Browser Use](entries/browser-use.md) — MIT-licensed Python/Rust framework giving LLM agents real browser control — useful for web scraping, form automation, and accessing bioinformatics portals, but Rust-core beta is still experimental and full capability requires commercial cloud
- [ChatBotKit](entries/chatbotkit.md) — Commercial AI agent platform with enterprise security (GDPR/CCPA), MCP integration, OAuth flows, observability dashboards, policy management, and modular architecture for composable datasets/skillsets/integrations; Forward Deployment platform for rapid AI product prototyping
- [ChemBERTa-3](entries/chemberta3.md) — ChemBERTa-3 is a training and benchmarking framework for chemical foundation models, providing a unified pipeline to pretrain, fine-tune, and evaluate transformer and graph-pretraining architectures on molecular property prediction tasks against MoleculeNet benchmarks
- [Chroma](entries/chroma.md) — AI-native open-source embedding database — 4-function core API, automatic tokenization/embedding/indexing, metadata filtering, in-memory or persistent modes, SIMD-accelerated maxscore, sharding, group-by aggregation, Python/JS/Go clients; Chroma Cloud for managed serverless; Apache-2.0
- [ChromaDB](entries/chromadb.md) — Open-source AI search infrastructure with vector, full-text, regex, and hybrid search — embedding-agnostic with Python/JS SDKs, serverless cloud option, and multi-modal retrieval; strong fit for literature/sequence RAG pipelines
- [Claude Mem](entries/claude-mem.md) — kb wiki + Claude Code built-in memory cover our needs; adds SQLite + Chroma + worker complexity without clear benefit
