- [HuggingFace Agents Course](entries/huggingface-agents-course.md) — Free framework-agnostic course on AI agent fundamentals — covers tool-calling loop mechanics (Thought/Action/Observation), stop-token control to prevent hallucinated observations, system prompt construction, and hands-on progression from dummy agents to smolagents/LangGraph/LlamaIndex
- [HuggingFace LLM Course](entries/huggingface-llm-course.md) — Free 12-chapter course covering transformers, fine-tuning, datasets, tokenizers, and reasoning models using the HuggingFace ecosystem; useful learning path but not a tool or workflow component
- [Internal Bot Leaked the Unannounced Reorg Plan (permission sprawl)](entries/internal-agent-permission-sprawl.md) — Incident writeup — an internal Q&A assistant scoped 'to read the wiki' answered with a confidential reorg spreadsheet including salary bands, because a broad Drive grant at setup indexed the HR folder; a follow-up audit found another wiki-summarizer bot holding inherited delete access and send-as-mail rights from its creator's account; thesis: over-broad access is itself the incident — 'an agent that can read everything will eventually read the thing it shouldn't'
- [Isaac Flath — Documents in AI Products (OCR Pipeline Guides)](entries/isaacflath-ocr-guides.md) — 14-post blog series covering OCR model selection, VLM OCR, speculative decoding, PDF retrieval, chunking, quantization, and document QA — practical guidelines for designing OCR pipelines
- [itch.io](entries/itch-io.md) — Open, non-curated marketplace and hosting platform for indie digital creations (primarily games, plus game assets, tabletop RPGs, and zines); founded by Leaf Corcoran, launched March 2013; "open revenue sharing" model (introduced March 2015) lets each creator set the platform's cut from 0%-100% of a sale (default split 10% platform/90% creator), with pay-what-you-want pricing supported for both free and paid listings; no pre-publication content review; hosted over 200,000 games by 2019 and over 1 million total projects by 2024; also hosts community game jams and charity bundles; commonly used as a lower-stakes, non-exclusive complement to Steam rather than a competitor.
- [Karpathy's 12 Rules for CLAUDE.md](entries/karpathy-12-rules.md) — All 12 rules adopted via setup-behavioral-baseline.sh — installs to ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md (Claude) and ~/.codex/AGENTS.md (Codex)
- [Karpathy's LLM Wiki Pattern](entries/karpathy-llm-wiki.md) — Foundational pattern doc for LLM-maintained personal knowledge bases — directly describes the architecture kb-wiki implements
- [KV Cache VRAM Sizing Formula for Local LLM Inference](entries/kv-cache-vram-sizing.md) — Practitioner reference for calculating KV cache memory: formula (2 × layers × kv_heads × head_dim × context_tokens × bytes_per_value), worked example showing Llama 3.1 8B at 128K context uses 16GB for cache alone (3× the 4.9GB Q4 weights); explains why GQA models (fewer KV heads) are dramatically cheaper at long context than MHA models, and why Ollama's default small num_ctx hides the cost until you raise it
- [Landlock LSM (org and ecosystem)](entries/landlock-lsm.md) — GitHub org for Landlock, the Linux security module (kernel 5.13+) letting unprivileged processes irreversibly restrict their own filesystem/network access — hosts rust-landlock and go-landlock libraries, the island CLI sandboxing tool (Rust, Apache-2.0), landlockconfig, kernel dev tree, test tools, and the landlock.io site
- [LLMs from First Principles (Doug Does) + AI-engineer fine-tuning AMA](entries/llms-from-first-principles-course.md) — Free, account-less interactive course site by a senior AI engineer (ex full-stack) — three project-based courses: train a character-level transformer on Shakespeare, fine-tune a model for SQL question answering, and ML fundamentals (prediction and error analysis), with in-browser exercises and downloadable Python labs; posted alongside an r/learnmachinelearning AMA whose practitioner heuristics include: fine-tuning is usually not worth it given cheap frontier APIs unless the task is simple or data is massive, then distill a teacher via SFT → DPO → RLVR on expert/user data; most AI-engineering work is backend software engineering and data cleaning; segment production inputs to track training-data bias
- [Local Model Picks (2026)](entries/local-model-picks-2026.md) — Practitioner-curated list of open-source models for local/offline use with RAM requirements — Llama 4 Scout (32GB Q4), Qwen 3.6 7B (16GB, coding), DeepSeek R1 8B (16GB, reasoning), Phi-4 3.8B (8GB, reasoning), Kimi K2.6 1T (API-only, 32B active, matches Claude), Pixtral (VLM), Granite 3 (enterprise/compliance), GPT-OSS 20B/120B
- [LoRA (Hugging Face PEFT conceptual guide)](entries/lora-peft-guide.md) — Hugging Face PEFT conceptual guide to LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation): fine-tune large models by training two small low-rank update matrices while the original weights stay frozen — drastically fewer trainable parameters, portable per-task adapters, and no added inference latency once merged. Covers LoraConfig parameters, merge/unmerge/unload utilities, and initialization options (Gaussian, LoftQ for QLoRA, rank-stabilized rsLoRA).
- [Machine Learning Learning Roadmap (2026)](entries/ml-learning-roadmap-2026.md) — Comprehensive practitioner/professor-authored learning path for ML — 5 stages totaling ~25-35 weeks at 8-10 hrs/week: Python (3-5w), math (6-10w, 6 sub-tracks from linear algebra to information theory), PyTorch (2w), ML foundations (10-14w, 5 units from perceptrons to evaluation), then specialization (NLP/CV/RL); emphasizes concept ordering as the primary blocker, spaced repetition for retention, and exercises over passive consumption
- [MCP 2026-07-28 Specification Update](entries/mcp-2026-07-28-spec.md) — Major MCP spec release (informally 'v2') — fully stateless architecture removing sticky-routing requirement, hardened auth model, 12-month deprecation policy, graduated extensions for server-rendered UIs and async tasks; stewarded by AAIF (Linux Foundation, 240 members); 250M SDK downloads/week
- [MCP Tunnels (Anthropic)](entries/mcp-tunnels.md) — Anthropic research-preview feature connecting Claude to MCP servers inside private networks — outbound-only tunnel via cloudflared + Anthropic proxy, no inbound firewall rules or public endpoints needed; completes the enterprise security story for Claude Managed Agents alongside self-hosted sandboxes
- [Microsoft MAI Models: Building a Hill-Climbing Machine](entries/microsoft-mai-models.md) — Microsoft AI blog announcement (Build 2026) of seven in-house MAI models across image, voice, transcription, reasoning, and coding, distributed via Microsoft Foundry plus OpenRouter/Fireworks/Baseten; introduces 'Frontier Tuning', letting enterprise customers fine-tune model weights on their own workflow-trace data via reinforcement-learning environments, alongside a Mayo Clinic co-developed healthcare model.
- [MIT OpenCourseWare (OCW)](entries/mit-opencourseware.md) — MIT's free online course catalog — 2,500+ courses across all disciplines including linear algebra, statistics, biology, computer science; useful as source material for learning-vault ingestion and structured self-study
- [MMEB — Massive Multimodal Embedding Benchmark](entries/mmeb-benchmark.md) — Comprehensive benchmark for evaluating omni-modality embedding models — V1 covers 36 image-text datasets across classification/VQA/retrieval/grounding; V2 adds video and visual document tasks (78 total); V3 extends to audio, text retrieval, and agent-centric tasks (190 total); introduced alongside VLM2Vec; TIGER-AI-Lab, ICLR 2025 → TMLR/COLM 2026
- [Model Sovereignty and Data Flywheel Strategy](entries/model-sovereignty-data-flywheel.md) — Practitioner argument for organizations owning fine-tuned models rather than depending on closed API providers — cites GPT 5.6 Sol government-approval access restriction, declining GPU costs, competitive data flywheels as moat, and negative-example datasets for hallucination reduction
- [Model Workspace Protocol (MWP)](entries/model-workspace-protocol.md) — Research paper proposing filesystem structure as agent orchestration; numbered folders + markdown prompts replace multi-agent frameworks for sequential workflows
- [Multi-Session Agent Management Strategies](entries/multi-session-management-strategies.md) — A Reddit discussion distilling practitioner strategies for managing 3+ concurrent AI coding agent sessions — tool-based multiplexing, terminal customization, agent-managing-agents, and session-count discipline — to avoid lost context and wrong-session mistakes
- [nanoGPT](entries/nanogpt.md) — Karpathy's canonical minimal GPT training repo (~600 lines total); deprecated Nov 2025 in favor of nanochat but remains the clearest educational reference for transformer training loops
- [On-Prem LLM Deployment Architecture (Hardware + Inference Stack Selection)](entries/on-prem-llm-deployment-architecture.md) — Community-sourced reference on selecting hardware (NVIDIA DGX Spark, RTX multi-GPU, Apple Silicon Mac Studio Ultra, AMD Instinct) and inference stacks (vLLM, SGLang, llama.cpp, MLX, Ollama, Open WebUI) for self-hosted LLM serving at 10–20 concurrent users; covers memory sizing, throughput data, and real deployment reports
- [Open Knowledge Format (OKF)](entries/open-knowledge-format.md) — Google's spec (v0.2) for agent-friendly knowledge representation using Markdown + YAML frontmatter; adds provenance/freshness/trust signals, reference agent with web-crawling enrichment, interactive graph visualizer; closely aligned with our wiki pattern
- [Open Weight Models That Matter (June 2026)](entries/open-weight-models-june-2026.md) — OpenRouter analysis of the four open-weight models that matter as of June 2026 — DeepSeek V4 Flash (best cost/performance, 79% SWE-bench, $0.05/$0.24 per M tokens), GLM 5.2 (#1 open-weight intelligence index, Opus-class planning), MiniMax M3 (multimodal image/video, 1M context, MiniMax Sparse Attention), NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Ultra (US-built, hybrid Mamba-2+Transformer MoE, 550B/55B-active, free route available)
- [OpenCode Go](entries/opencode-go.md) — Low-cost subscription from the OpenCode team ($5 first month, then $10/month) giving access to popular open coding models — GLM-5.2/5.1, Kimi K2.7 Code/K2.6, Qwen3.7/3.6, MiniMax M2.7/M3, DeepSeek V4 Pro/Flash, MiMo — through OpenCode or any agent; dollar-value usage limits, models hosted US/EU/Singapore with a stated zero-retention policy; set up via OpenCode Zen + an API key
- [Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B DGX Spark Setup](entries/ornith-dgx-spark-setup.md) — Deployment toolkit for serving Ornith-1.5-35B-A3B (35B MoE, 3B active) on a single NVIDIA DGX Spark (128GB unified memory) via vLLM 0.24 with multi-token prediction — includes multi-user API gateway with per-user token counting and Grafana dashboards; 79 tok/s single-user, 25 tok/s × 20 concurrent users (490 tok/s aggregate), 262K context; NVFP4 checkpoint (~22 GiB)
- [OSSU Computer Science](entries/ossu-computer-science.md) — Open Source Society University's free, self-taught CS curriculum modeled on undergraduate degree requirements (ACM/IEEE CS 2013 guidelines), assembled from free online courses (MIT, Harvard, Princeton, etc.); organized into Intro CS, Core CS (programming, math, tools, systems, theory, security, applications, ethics), Advanced CS electives, and a peer-evaluated final project; ~2 years at 20 hrs/week
- [Pandas Tutor](entries/pandas-tutor.md) — Browser-based tool that visualizes step-by-step how Python pandas code transforms DataFrames across chained operations like filtering, sorting, grouping, and joining.
- [Pi-hole](entries/pi-hole.md) — Network-wide DNS ad blocker — mature infrastructure tool, informational for lab network hygiene
- [Production AI Portfolio Checklist (What Senior Engineers Actually Look For)](entries/production-ai-portfolio-checklist.md) — Community-sourced checklist from senior/lead AI engineers on what distinguishes a production-grade AI portfolio project from tutorial copies — eval sets built from real failures, p50/p95 latency and cost-per-successful-task metrics, idempotent tool calls, prompt versioning tied to eval runs, traces of failed runs, and a 'what went wrong' section; consensus recommendation: build one narrow-domain agent (#2 deterministic workflow) over a routing gateway (#1) or fine-tuning demo (#3)
- [Production-Grade Agentic AI Platforms (2026 Evaluation)](entries/production-agentic-platforms-2026.md) — Practitioner evaluation of 5 agentic AI platforms for production use, ranked by orchestration, stateful workflows, human-in-the-loop, RAG, evals, observability, governance, and deployment flexibility: LangGraph (max engineering control), Microsoft Agent Framework (Azure enterprises), SimplAI (enterprise agent ops + governance), CrewAI (fast multi-agent dev), n8n (workflow automation + integrations); key lesson: 'can build an agent' ≠ 'can run agents in production' — observability, evaluation, governance, failure handling, deployment, and human-in-the-loop are the differentiators
- [Qwopus3.8-27B-Flash](entries/qwopus38-27b-flash.md) — Post-trained efficiency variant of Qwen3.8-27B (hybrid DeltaNet + attention architecture) focused on reducing unnecessary reasoning while preserving capability — +12.8% decoder throughput, MTP acceptance 66.1% to 80.7%, ~9.9% less total output with significantly fewer P95 runaway-reasoning cases; trades 1.45pp on MMLU-Pro (92.73% to 91.28%); 13/14 agent tasks passed at 99 tok/s on RTX 5090; trained with high-quality SFT (top 10% of 1.5M samples) + NVIDIA NeMo-RL GSPO; GGUF format
- [Reducto — When Is a Bigger Model Actually Better? (model routing framework)](entries/reducto-model-routing.md) — Reducto engineering-blog post (2026-08-13, Apurv Gandhi) framing document-model routing as a constrained optimization problem — a per-request 'model advantage' score (delta), a dimensionless cost-quality tradeoff parameter (lambda) that carries across model upgrades, an oracle frontier for benchmarking, and an AUC-based efficiency metric for grading a learned router against that oracle; reports 43.4%/56.6% split of low-gain vs. meaningful-gain requests across 8,276 paired evaluations.
- [Remote Control](entries/remote-control.md) — built-in feature; try for long sessions
- [Running Large MoE Models on Consumer Hardware (llama.cpp CPU-MoE Offload)](entries/large-moe-consumer-hardware.md) — Practitioner guide to running 176B MoE models (Qwen3.8-Flash-Next, 6B active) on a single 16GB GPU + 192GB RAM via llama.cpp --cpu-moe flag — attention on GPU, experts on CPU; ~16 tok/s; includes llama.cpp n-gram speculative decoding reference (--spec-type ngram-simple/ngram-cache, --lookup-cache-dynamic for SSD offload, tuning flags --spec-ngram-simple-size-n/m), q8_0 KV cache sizing at 262K context (~14GB), and memory budgeting guidance
- [Shift Left Security](entries/shift-left-security.md) — Security testing practice — move checks earlier in the delivery pipeline; SAST (static, e.g. CodeQL/Semgrep in CI), DAST (dynamic, e.g. OWASP ZAP in staging), pre-commit hooks (Semgrep on changed files), editor plugins (SonarQube); canonical example is SQL injection prevented by parameterized queries
- [Six AI Engineer Skills as Six Named Company Problems](entries/six-ai-engineer-projects.md) — Curriculum-as-project-list framing six AI engineering skills as named, production-shaped builds: RAG (Fieldnote — answers from the chart on page 112, not its caption), Agents (Casefile — claims triage with typed handoffs, cost ceiling, human before payout), LLMOps (Warmstart — 3-layer cache over 400k questions/month with measured false-hit rate), Evals (Groundtruth — legal-research harness failing the PR when recall drops), Fine-tuning (Slotfill — LoRA on 40k freight invoices benchmarked against the prompt it replaces), Guardrails (Doorman — recruiting agent surviving resume-embedded injection)
- [Smithery](entries/smithery.md) — Smithery is a central hub for discovering, installing, and hosting Model Context Protocol servers, with a 7,000+ server registry, a meta-MCP router toolbox, managed OAuth via the agent.pw vault, and both local-install and Smithery-hosted remote deployment
- [Software Cost Estimation Models (COCOMO II & Alternatives)](entries/software-cost-estimation-models.md) — Reference on algorithmic software cost-estimation models — COCOMO II's three sub-models and effort/schedule formulas, plus alternatives (Putnam/SLIM, Function Point Analysis, SEER-SEM, story points, Wideband Delphi, analogy-based) — and their fit for git-derived, zero-touch size metrics.
- [Staff Engineers: Shift from Solving Problems to Shaping Systems](entries/staff-engineer-systems-thinking.md) — LeadDev article (Monzo staff engineer) on the four moves that distinguish staff-level impact: find the research gap (the real problem behind the visible one), have conviction about the right solution (redesign at source vs. patching heuristics), design for belief not just correctness (alignment as a design problem), and make yourself unnecessary (spread the thinking pattern, not just the implementation)
- [Structure-Aware Rendering (Eye-Tracking Study)](entries/structure-aware-rendering.md) — Eye-tracking study (n=53) comparing how code rendering strategies (static, token-by-token, AST-based structured) affect programmer visual attention — structured rendering reveals code in syntactic chunks (outer blocks before inner details), inducing deeper processing and better structural awareness than character-based streaming; argues rendering is a first-class interaction primitive for AI coding tools
- [SunCalc](entries/suncalc.md) — Interactive web app showing sun position, sunlight phases, shadow length, and golden/blue hour times on a map for any location and date — useful for field work planning (outdoor sensor deployment, drone surveys, photography timing)
- [SWE-bench](entries/swe-bench.md) — Coding-agent benchmark built from real GitHub issues across (originally) 12 popular Python repositories: an agent generates a patch from the issue description and codebase, graded by human-developer-written unit tests split into FAIL_TO_PASS (the issue's own test must flip from failing to passing) and PASS_TO_PASS (all pre-existing tests must stay green). Variants: Full (complete dataset), Lite (lower-cost subset), Verified (500 human-annotated, solvable tasks, OpenAI-developed 2024), and Pro (Scale AI SEAL lab — harder, multi-file, cross-repository-structure tasks under a standardized 250-turn scaffold). By October 2026, SWE-bench Verified is widely considered no longer a reliable yardstick for frontier models (leaderboard stagnant since December 2025, contamination concerns), and even SWE-bench Pro had documented reward-hacking and task-quality issues (102 flagged instances), corrected in a 'Pro Verified' release. Earlier research (SWE-Bench+) found solution leakage in 18 Lite and 37 Verified instances. Derivative benchmarks address staleness: SWE-bench-Live (rolling weekly fresh issues) and SWEBench-verified-mini (50-issue reduced set).
- [TeamPCP/Miasma npm Supply Chain Attack (June 2026)](entries/teampcp-miasma-npm-supply-chain-attack-2026.md) — Incident writeup documenting credential-harvesting malware and ~/.claude/settings.json hook persistence — directly relevant to agent-lockdown hardening (WK-0031)
- [The AI Engineering Skills Map](entries/ai-engineering-skills-map.md) — DeepLearning.AI/Andrew Ng taxonomy of four core AI-engineering skills — building & deploying AI applications, software-engineering fundamentals, using coding agents, and shaping the build — synthesized from 10,000+ job postings, expert interviews, and surveys; skill 1 later expanded into six sub-components.
- [The State of Context Engineering 2026 (Redis)](entries/state-of-context-engineering-2026.md) — Redis-published survey report on context-engineering maturity among IT/AI infrastructure leaders: defines context engineering as deciding what an agent sees at each step (navigable, fresh, fast, compounding); reports 79% call context 'very important' yet 81% sit at the two earliest maturity stages (ad hoc/exploratory) and only 4% report reaching the 'compounding' stage; cites Gartner ('context engineering is in, prompt engineering is out', July 2025) and Cognizant's plan to train/deploy 1,000 context engineers (Aug 2025); promotes Redis's own Iris product and a gated 'Context Engineering Maturity Model' self-assessment/full report.
- [The State of Open Coding AI Models (August 2026)](entries/state-of-open-coding-models-2026.md) — Opinion snapshot (Aug 2026) arguing the best open coding models no longer fit regular consumer hardware, so 'open' no longer implies 'local.' Cites Kimi K3 (~2.8T params, ~1.56 TB on HF), GLM 5.2 (the one people called safe to leave running unattended; complaints about verbosity, not wrong answers), and DeepSeek V4 (vendor claims open-source SOTA on agentic coding, but the preview checkpoint felt rough and headline numbers came from the vendor's own agent harness). Thesis: the interesting engineering has shifted from training these models to serving them faster/cheaper. Author discloses a bias (works for a GPU cloud provider).
- [TurboQuant — Extreme KV Cache Compression](entries/turboquant.md) — Foundational Google Research algorithm (ICLR 2026) for 3-4 bit KV cache compression with zero accuracy loss; growing downstream ecosystem but no single canonical package yet
- [VCF vs Parquet for Genomic Variants (Reddit Discussion)](entries/vcf-parquet-discussion.md) — Former VCF spec maintainer explains why VCF persists: the real problem is representing complex variation (SVs, translocations, ploidy changes), not the container format — Parquet wouldn't solve schema complexity; VRS is the ongoing effort
- [Vector Database Basics: HNSW (Tiger Data / Timescale)](entries/vector-database-basics-hnsw.md) — Tiger Data (Timescale) blog explainer on HNSW (Hierarchical Navigable Small World) vector indexing: describes HNSW's skip-list-inspired multi-layer graph (sparse long-edge upper layers for coarse navigation, dense lower layers for fine-grained search), and its use with pgvector/Timescale Vector on Postgres via the m (max connections per layer) and ef_construction (build-time candidate list size) parameters that trade index size/build time for search accuracy.
- [Vector Database Cost Economics](entries/vector-database-cost-economics.md) — Practitioner cost model for vector databases: storage bytes = vector count × dimensions × precision (1M 768-dim float32 vectors ≈ 3GB before index/metadata overhead; dimensions scale storage linearly, so 3072-dim embeddings cost 4x what 768-dim cost); RAM-resident indexes cost roughly 10-50x more per GB than SSD, which is roughly 10x object storage (~$0.023/GB-month raw S3-class rate); production replication (2-3x) and HNSW-style index overhead (1.5-2x raw vector bytes) compound on top; query/write pricing (per-request, per-unit, or folded into storage) can dominate the bill at high QPS independent of storage cost.
- [Vector Embeddings Distance Metrics Cheat Sheet](entries/vector-distance-metrics-cheatsheet.md) — Practitioner cheat sheet on choosing vector distance metrics for RAG: cosine similarity (unnormalized text), dot product (fastest for normalized unit vectors), L2 Euclidean (clustering); plus Matryoshka trick — truncate embedding dimensions from 1536→512 to cut vector RAM by 80% with minimal accuracy loss
- [Verify Before Claim (Third Brain V5)](entries/verify-before-claim.md) — superpowers verification-before-completion covers this; confidence model is the novel addition
- [Vibe Coding Practitioner Habits (Pre-AI Developer Perspective)](entries/vibe-coding-practitioner-habits.md) — Experienced developer's framework for effective AI-assisted development: plan before prompting, break work into small pieces, read and understand generated code, ask 'why' before accepting fixes, maintain your own architectural mental model, challenge the AI on edge cases and alternatives, and learn fundamentals alongside it; core thesis is that AI becomes a force multiplier only when the developer can explain the system without AI — otherwise the AI starts leading instead of assisting
- [Windows 11 God Mode](entries/windows-god-mode.md) — Windows 10/11 trick — create a folder named GodMode.{ED7BA470-8E54-465E-825C-99712043E01C} to get a flat list of 200+ Control Panel shortcuts; useful for quick access to buried settings like environment variables, audio devices, power options
- [YouTubers for Agentic Workflow Building (community picks)](entries/agentic-workflow-youtubers.md) — Community-recommended YouTube creators for practical agentic-workflow content — Cody Schneider (outreach automation breakdowns), David Ondrej (n8n/make.com step-by-step with error handling), Matthew Berman (self-hosting agent frameworks incl. Docker setup and failure points), Nick Saraev (obscure automation triggers turned into business processes); requester also names Bilawal Sidhu, Greg Isenberg, Ras Mic as the desired non-salesy style
- [ZDR Router Trust — DeepSeek/Kimi/Xiaomi Data Leak Investigation (Sep 2026)](entries/zdr-router-trust.md) — Chinese authorities investigating DeepSeek, Moonshot (Kimi), and Xiaomi for routing third-party router users' requests to Anthropic models, injecting hidden prompts to harvest CoT traces, modifying returned text, and training on the interactions — raising questions about ZDR (zero data retention) credibility of third-party model routers like OpenRouter and OpenCode
- [Zero to Mastery ML](entries/zero-to-mastery-ml.md) — Comprehensive ML/DS course materials (NumPy, pandas, sklearn, TensorFlow); useful learning reference but not a tool or workflow component

### skill

- [ADHD](entries/adhd.md) — parallel divergent ideation via isolated cognitive frames; overlaps with superpowers:brainstorming but the frame isolation and scoring mechanics are worth studying
- [Advise Project Approach](entries/advise-project-approach.md) — structured project-level architecture research with comparable analysis, cost checks, and tradeoff discipline; complements /catalog (tool-level) with project-level advisory
- [Agent Session Resume](entries/agent-session-resume.md) — Cross-agent session resume skill with structured checkpoint workflow; overlaps with our handoff skill but adds multi-agent-platform support (Codex, Cursor, Antigravity, OpenCode)
- [anything2explainer](entries/anything2explainer.md) — Claude Code / Codex skill that turns any topic into a narrated motion-graphics explainer video (1280×720 H.264) — 9-stage pipeline: research → narration → TTS voiceover → storyboard → parallel build agents writing Remotion (React/TS) components per shot → QC; black-canvas white-line-art style, word-boundary-aligned subtitles, chapter progress bar; Chinese and English; 1–3 hours wall clock; PolyForm Noncommercial
- [Archify](entries/archify.md) — Agent skill that turns codebase descriptions or system architectures into polished, interactive HTML/SVG diagrams — five diagram types (architecture, workflow, sequence, data-flow, lifecycle), typed JSON IR, deterministic validation, delta comparison for PR review, share-card export; installs into Claude Code / Codex / Cursor / OpenCode; MIT
- [Astra Flash Orchestrator](entries/astra-flash-orchestrator.md) — Codex skill that reserves GPT-6 Astra for planning/architecture/review and routes discovery, implementation, testing, and reporting to DeepSeek V4.1 Flash via a native astra_flash_builder subagent role and an existing Codex Router route; author's one field build measured 98.9% less Astra input per 1K implementation lines ($10/$50 per-1M-token Astra pricing vs $0.15-0.30/$0.60-1.20 for Flash); installs a skill under ~/.agents/skills/, a pinned agent role under $CODEX_HOME/agents/, and a scoped policy block in $CODEX_HOME/AGENTS.md, with preview/apply, backups, and an undo receipt; MIT
- [Attention Control](entries/attention-control.md) — Output style / skill for coding agents applying air-traffic-control communication discipline (fixed message shape + controlled vocabulary) to agent prose, combining the shape rules from 'i-have-adhd' with the language rules from 'asd-ste100'; ships as a native Claude Code output style and as a skill/rules-file for Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot, and Zed.
- [blueprint4llm](entries/blueprint4llm.md) — CLI + Claude Code/Codex/Pi skill that renders Markdown plans (plan.md) to HTML or PDF with embedded diagrams and charts, offline, without calling an LLM — fenced blocks for D2, Mermaid, Graphviz, PlantUML, draw.io, Pencil, Balsamiq, Figma, OpenAPI, Excalidraw, Vega-Lite, and ECharts, each rendered locally to SVG; live preview server, CI validation (--check with JSON diagnostics), and a programmatic ESM API; also ships a VS Code preview/export extension; MIT, requires Node.js ≥22.13 and Puppeteer's Chrome.
- [Book to Skill](entries/book-to-skill.md) — directly useful for computational biology methods PDFs
- [Claude Skill Steelman](entries/claude-skill-steelman.md) — Single-purpose /steelman skill that pressure-tests decisions by arguing strongest alternatives at full strength — anti-sycophancy focused, context-specific; overlaps with doubt-driven-development (agent-skills) and superpowers:brainstorming
- [Claude Video (/watch)](entries/claude-video.md) — Agent Skill that lets Claude (and 50+ other skill hosts) 'watch' a video from a URL or local path: yt-dlp fetches captions/video, ffmpeg extracts scene-aware or keyframe images (with a dedup pass and duration-aware frame budget), a timestamped transcript comes from native captions or a Whisper fallback (Groq/OpenAI), and the frames + transcript are handed to the model to answer grounded in what's on screen and in audio.
- [Claudex-Loop](entries/claudex-loop.md) — Claudex-loop enforces a cross-model review invariant where Claude plans and Codex reviews in a read-only sandbox, through four phases — recon, interrogate, review, and optional build — producing a locked plan and a full round-by-round review transcript
- [Cloudflare Security Audit Skill](entries/cloudflare-security-audit-skill.md) — Six-phase coding-agent skill for structured security audits — reconnaissance, coverage-led hunting with isolated agents, adversarial candidate validation, structured JSON output with schema validation, independent record verification, and target-neutral reporting; seeds Cloudflare's internal vulnerability discovery harness; installs via `npx skills add`; MIT
- [Context Graph Compressor](entries/context-graph-compressor.md) — Structured JSON graph handoff with importance tagging and cross-LLM portability beats plain-text handoff skills; too new/single-contributor to adopt outright
- [daily-team (rotating four-person creative agent team)](entries/daily-team.md) — Claude Code skill and GitHub Actions project that generates a new four-member creative team each day — `team.sh` deterministically maps the date to four personas, each with a role, working method, stance (e.g. one cuts scope, one questions the brief, one speaks for the user) and, from season 3, a temperament, plus a constraint and an open 'spark'; a model plays the team, which proposes, objects, decides, and builds one small single-file HTML tool; scripts validate it (title, mobile layout, <100KB, no network requests), screenshot it, and commit the day record; weekly seasons rotate rosters with no-repeat windows; install as ~/.claude/skills/daily-team and run `/daily-team`; MIT
- [DeepSeek and Destroy](entries/deepseek-and-destroy.md) — Portable, multi-harness coding-agent skill in which an expensive orchestrator model decomposes and gates a multi-phase implementation plan while routing survey, implementation, review, and verification work to cheap DeepSeek-v4-flash workers via OpenCode CLI, using a worker proof-obligation discipline meant to catch tests that pass for the wrong reason.
- [Diagram Design](entries/diagram-design.md) — Editorial-quality diagram skill for Claude Code, Codex, Copilot, Factory Droid, Pi, and other Agent Skills hosts — 42 visual types (architecture, flowchart, sequence, state, ER, timeline, Sankey, Wardley map, exploded axonometric, etc.) as self-contained HTML+SVG; brand onboarding from a URL; import from draw.io/Mermaid/Excalidraw; export to SVG/PNG; 9 semantic behavior patterns; optional accessible motion; 87 monochrome icons; no shadows, no Mermaid; MIT
- [Finding Unknowns Skills](entries/finding-unknowns-skills.md) — 8 installable SKILL.md skills distilled (with attribution) from Thariq Shihipar's essay on finding your unknowns — blindspot-pass, brainstorm-prototypes, interview-me, reference-hunt, implementation-plan (before), implementation-notes (during), pitch-packager and change-quiz (after); each keys its trigger on the situation and the essay's literal phrases; MIT, cross-agent (Claude Code / Codex / any agentskills.io reader)
- [First Principles Destructor](entries/first-principles-destructor.md) — 6-stage assumption-destruction skill — inventories hidden assumptions, strips to physics/math/logic floor, calculates 'convention tax', removes non-load-bearing conventions, rebuilds from reality; multi-platform (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Cursor, Kiro); MIT
- [Grill With Docs](entries/grill-with-docs.md) — Well-structured DDD design-review skill that enforces domain language, but relies on companion format files (CONTEXT-FORMAT.md, ADR-FORMAT.md) not bundled with the skill.
- [GSAP Animation Expert Skill](entries/gsap-animation-expert.md) — Claude Code skill providing GSAP (GreenSock Animation Platform) animation expertise — timelines, ScrollTrigger, SVG morphing, stagger patterns, easing, and React integration via the useGSAP hook. Covers basic tweens, position parameters, horizontal scroll sections, text split animations, and scroll-reveal patterns. Single SKILL.md file. Attributed to Brookside BI.
- [HVE Core (Hypervelocity Engineering Core)](entries/hve-core.md) — Microsoft's open-source agentic SDLC framework for GitHub Copilot — specialized agents (research/plan/implement/review via an RPI methodology), coding-standard instructions applied automatically, and reusable skills (slash-command entry points) encoding ~80 organizational standards, security/compliance processes, and architecture-review practices; central principle 'AI carries the rules, humans keep the judgment.' Installed as a VS Code extension or Copilot CLI plugin (copilot plugin marketplace add microsoft/hve-core); explicitly flagged by Microsoft as a highly opinionated, rapidly evolving framework best treated as a source of patterns rather than a stable production dependency. MIT, with OWASP-derived skill content under CC BY-SA 4.0.
- [img2threejs](entries/img2threejs.md) — Image-to-3D reconstruction skill for Claude Code/Codex/OpenCode that turns a single reference image into a quality-gated, animation-ready TypeScript THREE.Group factory via a staged sculpting pipeline, using stdlib-only Python scripts for validation/gating so model tokens are spent only on visual judgment
- [Impeccable](entries/impeccable.md) — Impeccable provides design vocabulary and quality enforcement for AI coding agents, catching common AI-generated frontend tells through 45 deterministic detector rules plus 23 skill commands for shaping, critiquing, and polishing UI work, with provider-native hooks across Cursor, Claude Code, and other tools
- [Matt Pocock Skills](entries/matt-pocock-skills.md) — Collection of 17 composable Claude Code skills for disciplined engineering — grilling sessions (alignment interviews before coding), domain modeling (CONTEXT.md + ADRs), TDD red-green-refactor loop, codebase architecture audits with HTML reports, issue triage state machine, PRD synthesis, bug diagnosis loop, code review (standards + spec axes); installable via npx skills
- [Motion Design Skill](entries/motion-design-skill.md) — LottieFiles agent skill teaching universal, implementation-agnostic motion-design principles — timing, easing, choreography, and Disney's 12 principles adapted for UI — so agents choose motion intent before writing animation code. Works with any system (CSS, Framer Motion, GSAP, Lottie, Spring); ships philosophy, pattern recipes, and reference tables. Installs via npx skills add. MIT.
- [Obsidian Skills (Kepano)](entries/obsidian-skills.md) — Obsidian-specific; Defuddle (web→clean markdown) and skill packaging patterns worth noting
- [Papermorph](entries/papermorph.md) — Agent skill (Claude Code / OpenCode, invoked as /papermorph) that turns a textbook PDF or a story into a self-hosted static web book — narrated step-by-step lessons with graded quizzes, paper-puppet picture-book films, or documentaries/shorts/explorable pages; plans chapters, writes narration, animates visuals to per-word TTS timing, and self-reviews output before publishing; exports to MP4+SRT or SCORM; MIT license (bundled MathJax Apache-2.0, media assets keep own licenses); ~1,000 GitHub stars
- [PII Detection / Compliance Skills](entries/pii-detection.md) — HIPAA/CCPA/PCI-DSS coverage; pilot if handling sensitive data
- [Pilotfish](entries/pilotfish.md) — Multi-model orchestration layer for Claude Code — six agent roles (haiku scouts, sonnet executor, opus judgment, adversarial verifier, security) that delegate from a frontier orchestrator; one-prompt install to ~/.claude/, benchmarked at 96% of all-Fable performance for 46% cost
- [Reddit Pain Research Skill](entries/reddit-pain-research-skill.md) — Codex skill for evidence-based Reddit customer-discovery research — plans a study, collects and clusters pain-point evidence, and produces falsifiable monetization hypotheses via deterministic Python helpers, gated by a human approval checkpoint before evidence collection begins.
- [seo-god](entries/seo-god.md) — Claude Code skill that turns the agent into an SEO operator for a site you own — five-phase pipeline (setup → audit → measure → ai_visibility → schedule) using a local OpenSEO Docker container for crawling, Google Search Console for metrics, and a daily act loop that fixes regressions and improves near-ranking pages; no SERP scraping, no paid API keys required on the free path; MIT
- [Shader Skills](entries/shader-skills.md) — Agent skill (gokayfem) teaching Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Copilot, and Gemini CLI to write procedural GLSL/Shadertoy shaders that compile on the first try. Ships a 186-function GLSL library across 20 modules (hashing, noise, SDFs, raymarching, shading, water, volumetrics, color/tonemapping, cameras, debugging), each function WebGL2-compiled and GPU-tested (90 property tests across 16,384 samples), plus a Python bundler that inlines only the functions a given shader uses into one Shadertoy-ready file, and a checker that catches common WebGL-vs-desktop-GLSL mistakes. v2.0.0 tagged 2026-10-23; MIT; 410 stars, 36 forks at time of review.
- [Simon Says / Mother Goose (Agent Skills collections)](entries/simon-says-mother-goose.md) — Two companion MIT-licensed, explicit-invocation Agent Skills collections (SKILL.md-compatible) by a solo hobbyist author, targeting recurring AI-coding-agent failure modes — Simon Says covers scope/execution/investigation/testing/alignment drift (e.g. Patty Cake: diffs instructions vs plan vs completed work and stops on material drift); Mother Goose covers repair loops, verification, evidence, automation scope, and provenance (e.g. Pinocchio: requires evidence for completion claims; The Emperor's New Clothes: protected dissent against groupthink)
- [skills-visibility (Evil Martians)](entries/skills-visibility.md) — Agent skill (by Evil Martians) that walks through publishing a discoverable, installable catalog of agent skills: lay each skill out as skills/<name>/SKILL.md in a git repo, classify its shape (single-file / multi-file / bundle), re-host and hash the served bytes from a domain you control, build flat reproducible tar.gz archives, and publish a .well-known/agent-skills/index.json discovery index (agentskills.io schema / Cloudflare's RFC 8615 extension) so npx skills, gh skill, and Claude's plugin marketplace resolve them. Emphasizes sha256 integrity digests and offering multiple install commands.
- [Socratic](entries/socratic.md) — Self-interrogation skill packaging 697 engineering questions across 15 domains — agent silently works through relevant questions, reads codebase, applies defaults, and only escalates genuine authority decisions to user; Core (90 questions) and Full (697) modes; knowledge-backed packs for specialist depth; MIT
- [sota-scan](entries/sota-scan.md) — Useful comparative benchmarking skill for Claude Code, but single-contributor, no tests, and high token cost for exhaustive mode
- [subtask4llm](entries/subtask4llm.md) — Skill pack that splits a coding task into a dependency-ordered plan of up to 10 subtasks, each run by a fresh, isolated one-shot coding-agent worker (Pi Coding Agent or OpenAI Codex CLI) with its own clean context window instead of one agent accumulating the whole task's history; a Python runner (scripts/run_workflow.py) executes workers sequentially, passing each only shared project context plus its declared-dependency results, with bounded per-step timeouts/retries, one shared live log, and --continue-on-error; ships subtask4llm-pi and subtask4llm-codex skills installable independently via the Skills CLI (no Claude Code skill yet — README requests a PR); MIT
- [Superpowers: Verification Before Completion](entries/superpowers-verification-before-completion.md) — canonical 'evidence before claims' gate skill — already active in this project and referenced by other catalog entries as the standard
- [Taste Skill](entries/taste-skill.md) — Collection of portable SKILL.md files (design-taste-frontend and variants) that give AI coding agents layout/typography/motion/spacing guidance intended to reduce generic-looking generated UI, plus separate image-generation skills for design reference boards
- [Three.js Skills](entries/threejs-skills.md) — Skill bundle (CloudAI-X) shipping 10 Three.js SKILL.md sub-skills for AI coding agents: fundamentals, geometry, materials, textures, lighting, loaders, animation, interaction, shaders, and post-processing — each triggering on the matching 3D/WebGL task. Installs via npx skills add. MIT.
- [Tutorial Creator](entries/tutorial-creator.md) — good for knowledge retention and onboarding
- [UI/UX Pro Max Skill](entries/ui-ux-pro-max-skill.md) — comprehensive design system generation but focused on general web/mobile UI; evaluate when dashboard/report aesthetics become a priority
- [Unforget](entries/unforget.md) — scan-for-escaped-items pattern worth noting for future kb lint rule or audit command
- [Unslop Skills (UI / Text / Code)](entries/unslop-skills.md) — Three Claude skills that flag and remove AI 'tells' — unslop-ui (vibe-coded website patterns), unslop-text (AI prose cadences), unslop-code (AI code artifacts); backed by 3.2M-post Reddit analysis across 47 subreddits, with standalone CI-gating scanners; MIT
- [Web Artifacts Builder](entries/web-artifacts-builder.md) — use when markdown isn't enough for interactive reports/dashboards
